# DuckDB Data Agent - Comprehensive Codebase Analysis

## 1. Overall Project Structure

The project is organized as a **monorepo** with separate frontend (React + TypeScript) and backend (FastAPI + Python) that get bundled together in a single Docker container.

```
/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/
├── frontend/                          # React + TypeScript + Vite
│   ├── public/                        # Static assets (Langfuse icon)
│   ├── src/
│   │   ├── components/                # UI components (12 component files)
│   │   ├── agent/                     # Agent service (SSE handling)
│   │   ├── AgentContext.tsx           # Context-based state management
│   │   ├── App.tsx                    # Main app entry point
│   │   ├── types.ts                   # Shared TypeScript types
│   │   └── main.tsx                   # React DOM render
│   ├── vite.config.ts                 # Build config with API proxy
│   └── package.json                   # npm dependencies
├── backend/                           # FastAPI + Python
│   ├── app/
│   │   ├── main.py                    # FastAPI app setup & routes
│   │   ├── config.py                  # Environment variables
│   │   ├── database.py                # DuckDB singleton & operations
│   │   ├── agent.py                   # Agent loop & SSE streaming
│   │   ├── tools.py                   # MCP tool definitions
│   │   ├── tracing.py                 # Langfuse integration
│   │   ├── routes/                    # API route handlers
│   │   │   ├── chat.py                # /api/chat SSE endpoint
│   │   │   ├── query.py               # /api/query SQL execution
│   │   │   ├── tables.py              # /api/tables & upload
│   │   │   └── langfuse_status.py     # /api/langfuse/status
│   │   └── data/                      # Sample datasets (titanic.csv)
│   ├── pyproject.toml                 # Poetry dependencies
│   └── .env.example                   # Configuration template
├── Dockerfile                         # Multi-stage production build
├── Makefile                           # Dev commands
└── README.md                          # Documentation
```

---

## 2. Frontend Architecture

**Framework**: React 18 + TypeScript + Vite

**State Management**: React Context API (AgentContext) + Local Component State

### Key Components Structure:

#### **App.tsx** (Lines 1-223) - Main Application Entry Point
- **Loading State**: Fetches health check and loads tables on mount
- **Two Modes**: 
  - **Editor Mode**: SQL playground (default)
  - **Agent Mode**: AI-powered chat interface
- **Mode Toggle**: Button switches between modes
- **Key Props**:
  - `tables`: TableInfo[] - loaded tables
  - `queryResult`: QueryResult | null - current query result
  - `editorQuery`: string | undefined - SQL editor content

**Key Methods**:
- `refreshTables()` - fetches `/api/tables` to get loaded tables
- `handleFileUpload()` - uploads CSV to `/api/upload`
- `handleLoadSample()` - loads Titanic sample via `/api/upload/sample`
- `handleQueryExecute()` - executes SQL via `/api/query`
- `handleTableDelete()` - deletes table via `/api/tables/{name}`

#### **AgentContext.tsx** (Lines 1-243) - Conversation State Management
**Purpose**: Manages all agent/chat state and conversation flow

**State Variables**:
```typescript
messages: ChatMessage[]           // Array of user & assistant messages
isStreaming: boolean             // True while response is being streamed
sessionIdRef: string | null      // Persisted session ID for resuming conversations
textBufferRef: string            // Buffers incoming text chunks
flushTimerRef: ReturnType<setTimeout> | null  // Debounces text updates (50ms)
assistantIdRef: string           // ID of current assistant message
segmentsRef: ContentSegment[]    // Parsed segments (thinking/tool/answer)
currentTextRef: string           // Accumulates current text being streamed
```

**Key Callbacks**:
- `sendMessage(text)` (Lines 61-220): Initiates agent loop, creates user & assistant messages, manages streaming lifecycle
- `flushText()` (Lines 48-59): Debounced flush of buffered text to message
- `clearMessages()` (Lines 222-229): Clears conversation and aborts current request

**Event Flow** (via callbacks from agentService):
1. `onTextChunk` → buffers text, triggers flush timer
2. `onThinkingDone` → creates thinking segment, switches to answer phase
3. `onToolCall` → creates pending tool segment with input
4. `onToolResult` → merges result into pending tool segment
5. `onDone` → finalizes message, stores session_id for resume
6. `onError` → appends error to message

**Session Continuity**: 
- First response initializes `sessionIdRef` (Line 176)
- Subsequent requests pass `sessionId` to resume conversation
- Claude Agent SDK's `--resume` flag enables multi-turn state

#### **agent/agentService.ts** (Lines 1-122) - SSE Client & Event Parser
**Purpose**: Handles Server-Sent Events (SSE) streaming from backend

**Main Function**: `runAgentLoop(message, sessionId, callbacks, signal)`
- Makes fetch POST to `/api/chat` with SSE streaming
- Parses `event: <type>` and `data: <json>` format
- Calls appropriate callback based on event type

**Event Types Parsed** (Lines 74-121):
- `thinking` / `answer`: Text chunks → `onTextChunk(text)`
- `thinking_done`: Boundary marker → `onThinkingDone()`
- `tool_call`: Tool execution started → `onToolCall({id, name, sql/command, input})`
- `tool_result`: Tool completed → `onToolResult({id, columns, rows, error, output})`
- `done`: Conversation ended → `onDone(sessionId)`
- `error`: Error occurred → `onError(message)`

#### **Chat/Message Components**

**AgentPanel.tsx** (Lines 1-60)
- Container for agent chat interface
- Manages auto-scroll to bottom (Lines 16-18)
- Header with "Langfuse Traces" button (Lines 25-37)
- "Clear" button (Lines 38-42)
- Message list with MessageBubbles
- ChatInput for user input

**ChatInput.tsx** (Lines 1-55)
- Textarea with auto-height expansion
- Send button (Enter to send, Shift+Enter for newline)
- Disables during streaming

**MessageBubble.tsx** (Lines 1-155) - Complex Message Rendering
- Separates messages by role (user vs assistant)
- **Segment-based rendering** for assistant messages with segments:
  - `ThinkingBlock`: Collapsible `<details>` with thinking content and inline tools
  - Answer segments: Plain text answer
  - Tool segments: Rendered via InlineQueryResult
- **Fallback rendering** for non-segmented messages (legacy format)
- Dynamic `streamingRemainder` calculation (Lines 78-89) for incremental updates
- Animated "Thinking..." indicator during streaming

**InlineQueryResult.tsx** (Lines 1-121)
- Displays tool execution results inline
- Detects tool type (SQL, Bash, generic) with color-coded borders
- For SQL tools: renders columns, rows (capped at 20), row count
- For non-SQL: shows output text or raw content
- Pending state shows "Executing..."
- Error highlighting for failed executions

#### **Query Editor Components**

**QueryEditor.tsx** (Lines 1-64)
- Textarea for SQL input (6 rows, monospace)
- Ctrl/Cmd+Enter to execute
- "Run Query" button
- Tracks running state

**ResultsTable.tsx** (Lines 1-165)
- **Sorting**: Click column header to sort asc/desc/none (Lines 80-86)
- **Column Filtering**: Per-column text filter (Lines 88-90)
- **Global Search**: Search across all columns (Lines 100-106)
- **Display Cap**: Shows first 1000 rows, indicates if truncated (Line 9)
- **Summary Row**: "X rows in Yms" with filtering info

**ResultMarkdown.tsx** (Lines 1-30)
- For EXPLAIN queries (detected by backend based on SQL)
- Wraps output in code block and renders with ReactMarkdown

#### **Data Management Components**

**FileUpload.tsx** (Lines 1-100)
- Drag-and-drop or click to upload CSV
- File validation: .csv extension, max 500MB
- Two options: Upload custom file OR load sample dataset
- Shows uploading/loading state

**Sidebar.tsx** (Lines 1-84)
- Collapsible list of loaded tables
- Expandable table details (columns with types)
- Click table name → populates editor with `SELECT * FROM "table" LIMIT 100`
- Delete button for each table (with confirmation)
- Row count display

**ErrorMessage.tsx** - Error display component

### Type System (types.ts, Lines 1-59)

```typescript
interface ColumnInfo { name, type }
interface TableInfo { name, columns[], rowCount }
interface QueryResult { columns[], rows[], rowCount, executionTimeMs, resultType: 'table'|'markdown' }
interface ToolCallInfo { id, sql }
interface ToolCallResult { 
  toolCallId, toolName, sql, command, toolInput, 
  columns, rows, rowCount, error, output, rawContent 
}
interface ContentSegment { type: 'thinking'|'tool'|'answer', text?, toolResult? }
interface ChatMessage { 
  id, role: 'user'|'assistant', content, toolCalls[], 
  segments[], isStreaming, currentPhase 
}
interface LangfuseStatus { enabled, dashboardUrl }
```

---

## 3. Backend Architecture

**Framework**: FastAPI + Uvicorn (async)
**Database**: DuckDB (in-memory)
**AI Integration**: Claude Agent SDK (Anthropic)
**Observability**: Langfuse (optional OTel auto-instrumentation)

### Core Modules

#### **main.py** (Lines 1-44) - FastAPI App Setup
- CORS middleware: allows all origins (`allow_origins=["*"]`)
- Route registration: tables, query, chat, langfuse_status
- Health check: `GET /api/health`
- Frontend serving: In production, mounts static files and serves SPA fallback

#### **config.py** (Lines 1-13) - Environment Configuration
```python
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY = os.getenv("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", "")
ANTHROPIC_MODEL = os.getenv("ANTHROPIC_MODEL", "claude-opus-4-6")
LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY = os.getenv("LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY", "")
LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY = os.getenv("LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY", "")
LANGFUSE_BASE_URL = os.getenv("LANGFUSE_BASE_URL", "https://cloud.langfuse.com")
LANGFUSE_ENABLED = bool(LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY and LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY)
```

#### **database.py** (Lines 1-64) - DuckDB Singleton
**Singleton Instance**: `db = Database()`

**Key Methods**:
- `execute_query(sql)` (Lines 11-18): Execute SQL, return dict with columns, rows, rowCount
- `load_csv(file_bytes, filename, table_name)` (Lines 20-31): Load CSV into table
- `get_table_info(table_name)` (Lines 33-42): Get schema and row count for table
- `list_tables()` (Lines 44-48): Return info for all loaded tables
- `drop_table(table_name)` (Lines 50-52): Delete table
- `load_sample_data(csv_path, table_name)` (Lines 54-59): Load sample CSV from disk

**Connection**: In-memory DuckDB (`:memory:`)

### API Routes

#### **POST /api/chat** (routes/chat.py, Lines 15-25)
- Request: `ChatRequest { message: str, session_id: str | None }`
- Response: `StreamingResponse` with Server-Sent Events (SSE)
- Headers set for SSE compatibility
- Calls `stream_chat(message, session_id)` from agent.py

#### **POST /api/query** (routes/query.py, Lines 13-21)
- Request: `QueryRequest { sql: str }`
- Response: `{ columns, rows, rowCount, resultType }`
- Detects EXPLAIN queries → returns `resultType: 'markdown'` instead of `'table'`
- Error handling: HTTPException 400 with detail message

#### **GET /api/tables** (routes/tables.py, Lines 18-20)
- Returns list of all tables with schema info

#### **POST /api/upload** (routes/tables.py, Lines 23-30)
- File upload with automatic table name sanitization
- Sanitization: Remove .csv, lowercase, replace non-alphanumeric with _, ensure starts with letter
- Returns table info

#### **POST /api/upload/sample** (routes/tables.py, Lines 33-41)
- Loads built-in Titanic dataset from `app/data/titanic.csv`
- Returns table info

#### **DELETE /api/tables/{table_name}** (routes/tables.py, Lines 44-47)
- Drops specified table
- Returns `{ ok: true }`

#### **GET /api/langfuse/status** (routes/langfuse_status.py, Lines 8-13)
- Returns `{ enabled: bool, dashboardUrl: string | null }`

### Agent Implementation (agent.py, Lines 1-229)

**Core Function**: `async stream_chat(message: str, session_id: str | None) -> AsyncIterator[str]`

**Initialization** (Lines 61-79):
1. Creates DuckDB MCP server via `create_duckdb_server()`
2. Sets up `ClaudeSDKClient` with:
   - `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` from config
   - `system_prompt` built from loaded tables (Lines 23-43)
   - `mcp_servers={"duckdb": duckdb_server}`
   - `allowed_tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"]`
   - `resume=session_id` if resuming (Line 76) - enables multi-turn state

**System Prompt** (Lines 23-43):
- Instructs model to use execute_sql tool for queries
- Lists all currently loaded tables with column info
- Provides DuckDB-specific guidance

**Langfuse Tracing** (Lines 83-105):
- Conditional initialization if `LANGFUSE_ENABLED`
- Sets up observation context for the entire chat
- Propagates session_id for child spans
- Wraps entire agent execution

**Streaming Loop** (Lines 106-224):
1. Awaits `client.connect()` and `client.query(message, session_id=...)`
2. Iterates through response events via `client.receive_response()`

**Event Processing**:

- **StreamEvent** (Lines 118-148):
  - `thinking_delta` → emit `event: thinking`
  - `text_delta` → emit `event: thinking` (before tools) or `event: answer` (after tools)
  - `content_block_start` with `thinking` → set flag
  - `content_block_start` with `text` → emit `thinking_done` if was thinking
  - `content_block_start` with `tool_use` → set `has_tool_calls=true`

- **AssistantMessage** (Lines 150-167):
  - Extract ToolUseBlocks: emit `tool_call` event
  - If SQL tool: immediately execute query via `db.execute_query()` (Lines 170-177)
  - Emit `tool_result` with query results or error

- **UserMessage** (Lines 179-196):
  - Extract ToolResultBlocks from non-SQL tools
  - Emit `tool_result` event with output/error

- **ResultMessage** (Lines 198-203):
  - Extract session_id (used by --resume for next turn)
  - Emit `done` event with session_id
  - If error, emit `error` event

**Error Handling** (Lines 205-206):
- Catch exceptions, emit `error` event

**Finalization** (Lines 207-228):
- Update Langfuse trace with final session_id
- Flush Langfuse
- Disconnect client

### Tool Definition (tools.py, Lines 1-37)

**Tool**: `execute_sql(args: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]`

```python
@tool("execute_sql", "Execute a SQL query against DuckDB...", {"sql": str})
async def execute_sql(args):
    sql = args["sql"]
    result = db.execute_query(sql)  # Query execution
    truncated_rows = result["rows"][:MAX_RESULT_ROWS]  # Cap at 100 rows
    return {
        "content": [{"type": "text", "text": json.dumps(result_json)}]
    }
```

- Wrapped by `create_sdk_mcp_server()` to create MCP server
- Returns JSON content block with query results

### Tracing Integration (tracing.py, Lines 1-90)

**Initialization** (Lines 11-67):
- Only initializes if `LANGFUSE_ENABLED = bool(LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY and LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY)`
- Sets environment variables for LangSmith/Langfuse bridge
- Creates Langfuse client via `get_client()`
- Configures LangSmith with OTel enabled
- Enables Claude Agent SDK auto-instrumentation

**Logging Configuration**:
- Suppresses noisy logs from langsmith/otel integration

**API**:
- `get_langfuse_client()` → Returns client or None
- `get_langfuse_dashboard_url()` → Returns project traces URL

---

## 4. Conversation & Chat History Management

### Frontend State (AgentContext.tsx)

**State Structure**:
```typescript
messages: ChatMessage[] = [
  { id, role: 'user', content: "What's in the data?", ... },
  {
    id, role: 'assistant', content: "...",
    segments: [
      { type: 'thinking', text: "Let me explore..." },
      { type: 'tool', toolResult: { sql: "SELECT...", columns, rows } },
      { type: 'answer', text: "I found..." }
    ],
    isStreaming: false
  },
  ...
]
```

**Streaming Mechanics**:
1. SSE events arrive from backend
2. Text chunks are buffered in `textBufferRef` with 50ms debounce
3. When tool call detected, pending tool segment added with just `sql`/`command`
4. When tool result arrives, result merged into pending segment
5. When thinking ends, `segments` array created with `onThinkingDone()`
6. Answer text accumulated until `onDone()` finalizes

**Session Persistence** (Lines 46, 92, 175-176):
- `sessionIdRef` persists across turns
- First response receives session_id from backend
- Subsequent requests include session_id in request body
- Backend uses `--resume` flag to restore agent state across turns

### Backend State

**Per-Request State** (agent.py, Lines 106-115):
- No persistent conversation history on backend
- `session_id` parameter enables Claude Agent SDK to retrieve previous turns
- Agent SDK handles multi-turn state internally via session mechanism

**Database State**:
- DuckDB tables are persistent in-memory during session
- Persists across agent requests (singleton `db` instance)
- Clears when process restarts

---

## 5. Query Lifecycle - From Input to Display

### Editor Mode (SQL Query)

```
User Input: SQL text in QueryEditor
        ↓
handleQueryExecute() (App.tsx:59-91)
        ↓
POST /api/query with { sql }
        ↓
Backend: db.execute_query(sql) (database.py:11-18)
  - Executes SQL
  - Returns { columns, rows, rowCount }
  - Detects if EXPLAIN → sets resultType
        ↓
Frontend receives response
  - Stores in queryResult state
        ↓
Render ResultsTable or ResultMarkdown
  - Sortable columns
  - Per-column filters
  - Global search
```

**Data Structures**:
- Input: `string` (raw SQL)
- Transit: `QueryRequest { sql: str }`
- Response: `QueryResult { columns[], rows[], rowCount, executionTimeMs, resultType }`
- Rendered: Table with sorting/filtering

### Agent Mode (Natural Language Query)

```
User Input: "Show me the top 5 oldest passengers"
        ↓
sendMessage(text) (AgentContext.tsx:61-220)
  - Creates ChatMessage { role: 'user', content: text }
  - Creates empty assistant message
  - Calls runAgentLoop()
        ↓
POST /api/chat with { message, session_id? }
  - SSE stream opens
        ↓
Backend: stream_chat(message, session_id) (agent.py:61-229)
  - Builds system prompt from tables
  - Creates Claude Agent SDK client with resume=session_id
  - Agent SDK calls execute_sql tool with SQL
  - Query executed, results returned
  - Streams events back to frontend
        ↓
Frontend: runAgentLoop receives SSE events
  - thinking event → textChunk callback
  - thinking_done event → thinking segment created
  - tool_call event → pending tool segment with sql
  - tool_result event → merge results into segment
  - answer event → text accumulated
  - done event → finalizes message, stores session_id
        ↓
AgentContext updates messages array via callbacks
  - Thinking block appears (collapsible)
  - Tool invocations appear with results
  - Answer text accumulates
        ↓
MessageBubble renders segments
  - ThinkingBlock with tools inside
  - Answer section
  - InlineQueryResult shows SQL + table
```

**Data Structures**:
- Request: `ChatRequest { message: str, session_id: str | null }`
- SSE Events:
  ```
  event: thinking | answer | thinking_done | tool_call | tool_result | done | error
  data: { ... JSON payload ... }
  ```
- Tool Call: `{ id, name, sql, command?, input? }`
- Tool Result: `{ id, name?, sql?, columns, rows, rowCount, error?, output? }`
- Final: `ChatMessage` with segments array

---

## 6. State Management

### Frontend State Architecture

**Global/Context State** (AgentContext.tsx):
```typescript
messages: ChatMessage[]          // Conversation history
isStreaming: boolean             // Streaming indicator
sessionIdRef: useRef<string?>    // Session ID for resume
// Internal refs for streaming:
textBufferRef                    // Debounced text accumulation
flushTimerRef                    // 50ms debounce timer
assistantIdRef                   // Current assistant message ID
segmentsRef                       // Content segments during streaming
currentTextRef                   // Thinking/answer text accumulation
```

**Component State**:
- **App.tsx**:
  - `tables: TableInfo[]`
  - `queryResult: QueryResult | null`
  - `error: string | null`
  - `editorQuery: string | undefined`
  - `sidebarCollapsed: boolean`
  - `agentOpen: boolean` (mode toggle)

- **QueryEditor.tsx**:
  - `sql: string` (local SQL text)
  - `running: boolean`

- **ResultsTable.tsx**:
  - `sortConfig: { column, direction }`
  - `columnFilters: Record<string, string>`
  - `globalFilter: string`

- **Sidebar.tsx**:
  - `expanded: Record<string, boolean>` (which tables expanded)

- **FileUpload.tsx**:
  - `dragging: boolean`
  - `uploading: boolean`
  - `loadingSample: boolean`

- **ChatInput.tsx**:
  - `text: string`

**State Flow**:
1. App provides context via `AgentProvider` wrapper
2. Components consume via `useAgent()` hook
3. Props passed down for table list, refresh functions
4. Changes to messages trigger UI re-renders
5. Callbacks from agentService update context state

**Data Persistence**:
- Session ID stored in ref (survives message updates but not page reload)
- Tables cached in App state (refreshed after each action)
- No localStorage persistence (all ephemeral)

## 8. Architecture Patterns & Design Decisions

### Frontend Patterns

1. **Context API for Global State**: AgentContext holds conversation state, eliminates prop drilling
2. **Callback-Driven Updates**: agentService uses callbacks to drive state updates (onTextChunk, onToolCall, etc.)
3. **Refs for Streaming**: Uses useRef for text buffers, timers, IDs to avoid unnecessary re-renders during streaming
4. **Debounced Text Flushing**: 50ms timer (flushTimerRef) batches text updates to avoid excessive re-renders
5. **Segment-Based Rendering**: Message content split into thinking/tool/answer segments for flexible layout
6. **Responsive Components**: QueryEditor, ChatInput use local state; App manages page-level state

### Backend Patterns

1. **Singleton Database**: Single DuckDB instance per process, in-memory persistence within session
2. **Async Streaming**: `AsyncIterator[str]` for SSE streaming, enables efficient text streaming
3. **MCP Server Wrapper**: Tool exposed via Model Context Protocol for agent communication
4. **Stateless Handlers**: Each route handler is stateless; agent SDK manages session state
5. **Conditional Initialization**: Langfuse tracing only activates if env vars present (zero overhead)
6. **Event-Driven Streaming**: Agent publishes discrete SSE events for client to parse and handle

### Data Flow

1. **Request-Response**: Most endpoints follow req/res pattern (query, upload, tables)
2. **Server-Sent Events**: Chat endpoint streams events as they occur, reduces latency
3. **Session-Based Continuity**: session_id parameter enables agent SDK to resume conversation state

---

## 10. Key Integration Points

### Frontend → Backend Communication

| Operation | Endpoint | Method | Body | Response |
|-----------|----------|--------|------|----------|
| Health check | `/api/health` | GET | - | `{ status }` |
| List tables | `/api/tables` | GET | - | `TableInfo[]` |
| Execute SQL | `/api/query` | POST | `{ sql }` | `QueryResult` |
| Chat/Agent | `/api/chat` | POST | `{ message, session_id? }` | SSE stream |
| Upload CSV | `/api/upload` | POST | FormData | `TableInfo` |
| Load sample | `/api/upload/sample` | POST | - | `TableInfo` |
| Delete table | `/api/tables/{name}` | DELETE | - | `{ ok }` |
| Langfuse status | `/api/langfuse/status` | GET | - | `LangfuseStatus` |

### SSE Event Types

```
event: thinking      | text chunk from thinking block
event: answer        | text chunk from answer block  
event: thinking_done | boundary between thinking and answer
event: tool_call     | tool execution initiated {id, name, sql, input}
event: tool_result   | tool completed {id, columns, rows, error}
event: done          | conversation complete {session_id}
event: error         | error occurred {message}
```

---

## Summary

The DuckDB Data Agent is a well-architected full-stack application that elegantly balances **SQL playground** functionality with **AI-powered natural language** capabilities. Key strengths:

- **Clean Separation**: Frontend/backend clearly separated with well-defined API contracts
- **Streaming-First Design**: SSE for real-time feedback, context-based state for efficient updates  
- **Multi-Turn Intelligence**: Session resumption via Agent SDK enables stateful conversations
- **Observable**: Langfuse integration (optional, zero overhead if disabled)
- **Modular Components**: Each UI component has single responsibility
- **Type-Safe**: Full TypeScript frontend with clear interfaces
- **Extensible**: MCP tool pattern makes adding tools straightforward

The conversation flow is particularly elegant: streaming text chunks are buffered and debounced, tool executions are shown inline as they happen, and thinking is encapsulated in collapsible segments—all contributing to a responsive, transparent user experience.

## Summary: Claude Agent SDK Session State Management

Based on my investigation of the Claude Agent SDK (version 0.1.39) and the `duckdb-data-agent` project, here are my findings:

### 1. SDK Import and Usage in `backend/app/agent.py`

The project uses the **`claude-agent-sdk`** Python package, imported as:
```python
from claude_agent_sdk import (
    ClaudeSDKClient,
    ClaudeAgentOptions,
    AssistantMessage,
    UserMessage,
    ResultMessage,
    ToolUseBlock,
    ToolResultBlock,
)
```

The SDK version is **0.1.39** (specified in `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/pyproject.toml`).

**Session Usage in agent.py:**
- Line 76: Sessions are resumed via the `resume` parameter in `ClaudeAgentOptions`
- Line 108: Session IDs are passed to `client.query()` method
- Line 200: The actual session ID is extracted from `ResultMessage.session_id` returned by the CLI

### 2. Session Storage Format and Location

**Sessions are stored as JSONL files** (JSON Lines format - one JSON object per line) in:
```
~/.claude/projects/<PATH_ENCODING>/
```

For the duckdb-data-agent project:
```
~/.claude/projects/-Users-cyyeh-Desktop-duckdb-data-agent/
```

**Session file structure:**
- **Filename:** `<session-uuid>.jsonl` (e.g., `092027de-37f1-44c9-89b9-adcbc36d087d.jsonl`)
- **Directory entries:** Sessions have both `.jsonl` files and corresponding directories
- **Memory directory:** `~/.claude/projects/-Users-cyyeh-Desktop-duckdb-data-agent/memory/` (for transient session data)

**Example session messages contain:**
- `type`: Message type (user, assistant, tool_use, tool_result, thinking, etc.)
- `uuid`: Unique message identifier
- `sessionId`: Session UUID
- `message`: The actual message content with role and content fields
- `timestamp`: ISO 8601 timestamp
- `parentUuid`: Reference to parent message
- `version`: Claude Code CLI version
- `gitBranch`: Current git branch
- `cwd`: Working directory

### 3. Session Management APIs

The SDK provides the following session-related functionality:

**In `ClaudeAgentOptions`:**
```python
resume: str | None = None                      # Resume existing session by ID
fork_session: bool = False                     # Fork to new session instead of continuing
enable_file_checkpointing: bool = False        # Track file changes during session
```

**In `ClaudeSDKClient`:**
```python
async def rewind_files(user_message_id: str) -> None
```
- Rewinds tracked files to their state at a specific user message
- Requires `enable_file_checkpointing=True`
- Requires `extra_args={"replay-user-messages": None}` to receive UserMessage UUIDs

### 4. Can We Truncate or Rewind Sessions?

**Direct API Support:**
- **File Rewinding:** YES - `ClaudeSDKClient.rewind_files()` allows rewinding file state to a specific message point
- **Message Truncation:** NO - The SDK does not provide direct APIs to truncate conversation history
- **Session Modification:** NO - The SDK does not expose APIs to modify or truncate session messages

**What the SDK CAN do:**
1. Resume an existing session: `ClaudeAgentOptions(resume="session-id")`
2. Fork a session (create new session from existing): `ClaudeAgentOptions(resume="session-id", fork_session=True)`
3. Rewind file state: `await client.rewind_files(user_message_id)`
4. Get file checkpoints: Via `enable_file_checkpointing=True` and `replay-user-messages` extra args

**What the SDK CANNOT do (through public APIs):**
1. Delete messages from a session
2. Truncate conversation history
3. Directly read/write JSONL session files
4. Pre-populate a new session with custom message history
5. Manipulate session state files directly

### 5. Session State Transmission to Claude Code CLI

The `--resume` flag is passed to the Claude Code CLI (subprocess):
```python
# In SubprocessCLITransport._build_command()
if self._options.resume:
    cmd.extend(["--resume", self._options.resume])
```

The actual session management (loading, reading history) is handled by the Claude Code CLI binary, not the Python SDK. The CLI is responsible for:
- Loading session files from `~/.claude/projects/`
- Managing conversation history
- Creating new sessions
- Enforcing session constraints

### 6. Possible Workarounds for Session Manipulation

If you need to modify sessions, you would need to:

1. **Directly manipulate JSONL files** (not recommended):
   - Read session JSONL: Parse each line as JSON
   - Filter/remove messages programmatically
   - Write back to file (with potential data loss risks)

2. **Create new sessions from scratch**:
   - Generate a new session UUID
   - Construct JSONL messages matching the expected format
   - Place in `~/.claude/projects/<path>/` directory
   - Use `resume` with the new session ID

3. **Upstream to Claude Code CLI**:
   - The actual limitation is in the Claude Code CLI, not the SDK
   - Session history manipulation features would need to be implemented at the CLI level
   - File paths: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/claude_agent_sdk/`

### Key Files and References

1. **SDK Client Implementation:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/claude_agent_sdk/client.py`
2. **Internal Query Handler:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/claude_agent_sdk/_internal/query.py`
3. **CLI Transport:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/claude_agent_sdk/_internal/transport/subprocess_cli.py`
4. **Type Definitions:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/claude_agent_sdk/types.py`
5. **Project Usage:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py` (lines 68-108)
6. **Session Storage:** `~/.claude/projects/-Users-cyyeh-Desktop-duckdb-data-agent/*.jsonl`

Great findings. Here's what I discovered about the Agent SDK session management:

**Key facts:**
- Sessions are stored as **JSONL files** (one JSON object per line) at `~/.claude/projects/<path>/<session-uuid>.jsonl`
- Each message entry has `uuid`, `parentUuid`, `timestamp`, and the full message content
- The SDK supports `fork_session=True` (creates a new session from existing), but it forks from the *current* state, not a specific point
- **No built-in API** to truncate/rewind conversation history

**Viable approaches for session manipulation:**

1. **JSONL truncation**: Copy session JSONL up to the edit point into a new file, then `resume` from it with the edited message
2. **Fresh session with conversation context**: Start a new session, inject prior conversation into the system prompt

Good, I have all the clarifications needed. Let me now propose approaches. Here are the key requirements summarized:

- **Modify**: Edit user message in-place, discard everything after, re-send edited message for fresh response
- **Delete**: Remove user message + everything after it (rewind)
- **UX**: Hover icons (pencil/trash) on user message bubbles
- **Session**: JSONL truncation + resume

---

## Approach 1: Separate Truncation Endpoint + Reuse Chat

**Backend**: New `POST /api/chat/truncate` endpoint that:
- Reads the session JSONL file, identifies the Nth user message entry, truncates everything from that point onward
- Returns success

**Frontend workflow**:
- **Edit**: Call `/api/chat/truncate`, update local messages, then call existing `/api/chat` with edited message + same session_id
- **Delete**: Call `/api/chat/truncate`, update local messages, done

**Pros**: Clean separation of concerns, reuses existing chat endpoint, truncation is idempotent
**Cons**: Two sequential API calls for edit (truncate then chat), slight race condition window

---

## Approach 2: Combined Edit/Delete Endpoints

**Backend**: Two new endpoints:
- `POST /api/chat/edit` — truncates JSONL + immediately streams fresh response (SSE), single request
- `POST /api/chat/delete` — truncates JSONL, returns success

**Frontend workflow**:
- **Edit**: Single call to `/api/chat/edit` that returns SSE stream (same as current chat)
- **Delete**: Single call to `/api/chat/delete`

**Pros**: Atomic edit operation (no race conditions), cleaner frontend flow
**Cons**: More backend code, edit endpoint duplicates some chat logic

---

## Approach 3: Extend Existing Chat Endpoint

**Backend**: Extend `POST /api/chat` request body with optional `truncate_before_index` field:
- If present, truncate session JSONL first, then proceed with the message as usual
- Delete gets a separate lightweight endpoint

**Pros**: Minimal new endpoints, leverages existing streaming infrastructure
**Cons**: Overloads the chat endpoint's responsibility

---

**My recommendation: Approach 2** — Combined endpoints give the cleanest UX with atomic operations and clear intent. The slight code duplication is worth the reliability.

## JSONL Session File Format Analysis - Claude Code Agent SDK

I've examined the actual JSONL session files stored in `~/.claude/projects/-Users-cyyeh-Desktop-duckdb-data-agent/`. Here's the complete format specification:

### File Location and Structure

Session files are stored at: `/Users/cyyeh/.claude/projects/-Users-cyyeh-Desktop-duckdb-data-agent/`

Each line is a valid JSON object representing one entry/event in the session.

### Entry Type Distribution

From the main session file (`523d3310-e91c-4cb5-b996-d7c2b1584670.jsonl` - 22 entries):

| Type                  | Count | Purpose |
|----------------------|-------|---------|
| `user`                | 4     | User messages (queries and tool results) |
| `assistant`           | 10    | Assistant responses (text, thinking, tool calls) |
| `progress`            | 6     | Hook/callback progress events |
| `system`              | 1     | System events (session start/stop hooks) |
| `file-history-snapshot` | 1   | File tracking snapshots |

### User Message Format

**Type:** `"type": "user"`

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "<uuid_of_previous_message>",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "userType": "external",
  "cwd": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent",
  "sessionId": "523d3310-e91c-4cb5-b996-d7c2b1584670",
  "version": "2.1.47",
  "gitBranch": "main",
  "type": "user",
  "message": {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "do I need to install claude cli in server runtime..."
  },
  "uuid": "8560da7c-e2d6-4cba-8b38-1ed2ba27d4f7",
  "timestamp": "2026-02-19T16:32:14.848Z",
  "todos": [],
  "permissionMode": "default"
}
```

**Key characteristics:**
- `message.role` = `"user"`
- `message.content` = string (the user's query)
- May have optional fields: `sourceToolAssistantUUID`, `toolUseResult`
- `todos` = array (usually empty)
- `permissionMode` = "default" or other values

### Assistant Message Format

**Type:** `"type": "assistant"`

Can contain **text**, **thinking**, or **tool_use** blocks:

#### Text Response Example:
```json
{
  "parentUuid": "8560da7c-e2d6-4cba-8b38-1ed2ba27d4f7",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "userType": "external",
  "cwd": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent",
  "sessionId": "523d3310-e91c-4cb5-b996-d7c2b1584670",
  "version": "2.1.47",
  "gitBranch": "main",
  "message": {
    "model": "claude-opus-4-6",
    "id": "msg_01SbXcBc6XCVzSxHwym6A5qW",
    "type": "message",
    "role": "assistant",
    "content": [
      {
        "type": "text",
        "text": "No, you don't need to install the Claude CLI..."
      }
    ],
    "stop_reason": null,
    "stop_sequence": null,
    "usage": {
      "input_tokens": 3,
      "cache_creation_input_tokens": 6080,
      "cache_read_input_tokens": 16313,
      "output_tokens": 14,
      "service_tier": "standard"
    }
  },
  "requestId": "REDACTED",
  "type": "assistant",
  "uuid": "adf38c32-56cd-4c08-a4c8-b37c0c8e7575",
  "timestamp": "2026-02-19T16:32:17.716Z"
}
```

#### Tool Use Example:
```json
{
  "message": {
    "model": "claude-opus-4-6",
    "id": "msg_01SbXcBc6XCVzSxHwym6A5qW",
    "type": "message",
    "role": "assistant",
    "content": [
      {
        "type": "tool_use",
        "id": "toolu_01H3EK3cEQUCktYCRHWpNaUx",
        "name": "Read",
        "input": {
          "file_path": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/Dockerfile"
        },
        "caller": {
          "type": "direct"
        }
      }
    ],
    "usage": {
      "output_tokens": 307,
      "server_tool_use": {
        "web_search_requests": 0,
        "web_fetch_requests": 0
      }
    }
  },
  "type": "assistant",
  "uuid": "af95ef6f-5496-4ea9-b83c-b8f816f0ab6b",
  "timestamp": "2026-02-19T16:32:23.378Z"
}
```

**Key characteristics:**
- `message.role` = `"assistant"`
- `message.content` = array of content blocks
- Content block types: `"text"`, `"thinking"`, `"tool_use"`
- `message.usage` contains token counts
- Tool use blocks have `id`, `name`, and `input` fields

### Progress Entry Format

**Type:** `"type": "progress"`

Represents hook/callback execution:

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "6826b002-a639-4709-a74f-cc8093656bc5",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "userType": "external",
  "cwd": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent",
  "sessionId": "523d3310-e91c-4cb5-b996-d7c2b1584670",
  "version": "2.1.47",
  "gitBranch": "main",
  "type": "progress",
  "data": {
    "type": "hook_progress",
    "hookEvent": "PostToolUse",
    "hookName": "PostToolUse:Read",
    "command": "callback"
  },
  "parentToolUseID": "toolu_01H3EK3cEQUCktYCRHWpNaUx",
  "toolUseID": "toolu_01H3EK3cEQUCktYCRHWpNaUx",
  "timestamp": "2026-02-19T16:32:23.405Z",
  "uuid": "f2301770-5798-43ed-a38c-f59dc54db197"
}
```

### System Entry Format

**Type:** `"type": "system"`

Represents system events:

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "1adefd93-48be-485e-9e7a-4bc80fc0840e",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "userType": "external",
  "cwd": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent",
  "sessionId": "523d3310-e91c-4cb5-b996-d7c2b1584670",
  "version": "2.1.47",
  "gitBranch": "main",
  "slug": "golden-weaving-spark",
  "type": "system",
  "subtype": "stop_hook_summary",
  "hookCount": 1,
  "hookInfos": [{"command": "entire hooks claude-code stop"}],
  "hookErrors": [],
  "preventedContinuation": false,
  "stopReason": "",
  "hasOutput": true,
  "level": "suggestion",
  "timestamp": "2026-02-19T16:32:44.334Z",
  "uuid": "d8642e68-6897-4c46-8273-4b8ba9992240"
}
```

### File History Snapshot Format

**Type:** `"type": "file-history-snapshot"`

Tracks file modifications:

```json
{
  "type": "file-history-snapshot",
  "messageId": "8560da7c-e2d6-4cba-8b38-1ed2ba27d4f7",
  "snapshot": {
    "messageId": "8560da7c-e2d6-4cba-8b38-1ed2ba27d4f7",
    "trackedFileBackups": {},
    "timestamp": "2026-02-19T16:32:15.214Z"
  },
  "isSnapshotUpdate": false
}
```

### Distinguishing User Query Messages

**Key to identifying an initial user query (NOT a tool result):**

1. **Check the `message` field structure:**
   - If `message.content` is a **string**: It's a user query
   - If `message.content` is an **array of objects**: It's likely a tool result response

2. **Check for `toolUseResult` field:**
   - Present = Tool result being submitted
   - Absent = User query

3. **Check `sourceToolAssistantUUID` field:**
   - Present = Tool result message
   - Absent = User query

4. **Check parent chain:**
   - If parent points to a `tool_use` assistant message: It's a tool result
   - If parent points to a generic assistant message or hook: It's a user query

### Parent-Child Relationship for Truncation

Sessions form a linked list via `parentUuid`:

```
Progress (hook) 
  → User Query ("do I need...")
    → Assistant (thinking)
      → Assistant (text response)
        → Assistant (tool_use: Read)
          → Progress (PostToolUse callback)
            → User Message (tool result)
              → Assistant (text response)
                → Progress (Stop hook)
```

### Critical Fields for Session Truncation

| Field | Purpose | Type |
|-------|---------|------|
| `uuid` | Unique identifier for this entry | string |
| `parentUuid` | Links to previous entry | string or null |
| `type` | Entry type | "user", "assistant", "progress", "system", "file-history-snapshot" |
| `message.role` | "user" or "assistant" | string |
| `message.content` | String for user queries, array for responses | string or array |
| `timestamp` | ISO 8601 timestamp | string |
| `sessionId` | Session identifier | string |
| `toolUseID` / `parentToolUseID` | Links tool invocations | string |

This format is consistent across `/Users/cyyeh/.claude/projects/-Users-cyyeh-Desktop-duckdb-data-agent/` and all subagent sessions.
agentId: ac3227f (for resuming to continue this agent's work if needed)
<usage>total_tokens: 47832
tool_uses: 23
duration_ms: 162418</usage>
```

## Section 1: Data Flow

### Edit Flow
```
User hovers message → clicks pencil icon → message enters edit mode (textarea)
    → user modifies text → clicks "Save & Resend"
    → Frontend: truncate messages array at edit index
    → POST /api/chat/edit { session_id, user_message_index, new_message }
    → Backend: find JSONL file, truncate at Nth user query entry
    → Backend: resume session with edited message, stream SSE response
    → Frontend: receive SSE stream (same as normal chat)
    → New assistant response appears
```

### Delete Flow
```
User hovers message → clicks trash icon → confirmation prompt
    → POST /api/chat/delete { session_id, user_message_index }
    → Backend: find JSONL file, truncate at Nth user query entry
    → Backend: return success
    → Frontend: truncate messages array at delete index
```

### JSONL Truncation Logic
The session JSONL has entries of types: `user`, `assistant`, `progress`, `system`, `file-history-snapshot`. User queries specifically have `type: "user"` with `message.content` as a **string** (not an array — tool results have arrays). To truncate before the Nth user query:
1. Read all JSONL lines
2. Walk through, counting entries where `type == "user"` and `message.content` is a string
3. When the count reaches the target index, note the line number
4. Write back only lines before that point

## Section 2: Backend API

### New Endpoints

**`POST /api/chat/edit`**
```python
class ChatEditRequest(BaseModel):
    session_id: str          # Current session ID
    user_message_index: int  # 0-based index of the user query to replace
    new_message: str         # The edited message text

# Response: SSE stream (same format as /api/chat)
```

Logic:
1. Validate `session_id` exists as a JSONL file
2. Call `truncate_session(session_id, user_message_index)` — removes everything from the Nth user query onward
3. Call `stream_chat(new_message, session_id)` — resumes the truncated session with the new message
4. Return SSE streaming response

**`POST /api/chat/delete`**
```python
class ChatDeleteRequest(BaseModel):
    session_id: str          # Current session ID
    user_message_index: int  # 0-based index of the user query to delete from

# Response: JSON { ok: true }
```

Logic:
1. Validate `session_id` exists
2. Call `truncate_session(session_id, user_message_index)`
3. Return `{ "ok": true }`

### Session Truncation Utility

New file `backend/app/session.py`:
```python
def truncate_session(session_id: str, user_message_index: int) -> None:
    """Truncate session JSONL file, removing the Nth user query and everything after."""
    session_path = _get_session_path(session_id)
    
    with open(session_path, 'r') as f:
        lines = f.readlines()
    
    user_query_count = 0
    truncate_at = len(lines)  # default: no truncation
    
    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        entry = json.loads(line)
        if _is_user_query(entry):
            if user_query_count == user_message_index:
                truncate_at = i
                break
            user_query_count += 1
    
    # Write truncated file
    with open(session_path, 'w') as f:
        f.writelines(lines[:truncate_at])

def _is_user_query(entry: dict) -> bool:
    """Check if a JSONL entry is a user query (not a tool result)."""
    return (
        entry.get("type") == "user" 
        and isinstance(entry.get("message", {}).get("content"), str)
    )

def _get_session_path(session_id: str) -> Path:
    """Construct path to session JSONL file."""
    cwd = os.getcwd()  # or project root
    encoded = cwd.replace("/", "-")
    return Path.home() / ".claude" / "projects" / encoded / f"{session_id}.jsonl"
```

### Route File: `backend/app/routes/chat.py` (extended)

Add two new route handlers alongside the existing `chat` endpoint, both calling the shared `truncate_session` utility.

## Section 3: Frontend State Management

### AgentContext Changes

**New state:**
```typescript
// No new state variables needed - we reuse existing messages/isStreaming
```

**New callbacks exposed via context:**

```typescript
editMessage(messageIndex: number, newContent: string): void
deleteMessage(messageIndex: number): void
```

**`editMessage` logic:**
1. Guard: if `isStreaming`, return (can't edit while streaming)
2. Truncate `messages` to `messages.slice(0, messageIndex)` — removes the edited message and everything after
3. Call `runAgentLoop` variant that POSTs to `/api/chat/edit` with `{ session_id, user_message_index, new_message }`
4. The user_message_index is computed by counting user messages in `messages.slice(0, messageIndex)` — i.e., which Nth user message this is
5. Create new user message with edited content + empty assistant message (same as `sendMessage`)
6. Stream response via SSE (reuse existing SSE callbacks)

**`deleteMessage` logic:**
1. Guard: if `isStreaming`, return
2. Compute `user_message_index` (count user messages before this one)
3. POST to `/api/chat/delete` with `{ session_id, user_message_index }`
4. On success: truncate `messages` to `messages.slice(0, messageIndex)`
5. If `messageIndex === 0` (first message deleted), clear `sessionIdRef` too (no session left)

**`agentService.ts` changes:**

New function:
```typescript
function runAgentEditLoop(
  sessionId: string,
  userMessageIndex: number, 
  newMessage: string,
  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
  signal: AbortSignal
): void
```
- Same SSE parsing logic as `runAgentLoop`
- POSTs to `/api/chat/edit` instead of `/api/chat`

New function:
```typescript
async function deleteAgentMessage(
  sessionId: string,
  userMessageIndex: number
): Promise<void>
```
- Simple fetch POST to `/api/chat/delete`

## Section 4: UI Components

### MessageBubble Changes

**Hover actions on user messages:**

When hovering over a user message bubble (right-aligned), show two small icon buttons:
- Pencil icon (edit) — top-right of the bubble
- Trash icon (delete) — next to pencil

**Edit mode:**

When pencil is clicked, the user message transforms from static text into an editable textarea:
- Pre-populated with the current message content
- Auto-focused, same styling as the bubble but with a border indicating edit mode
- Two buttons below: "Save & Resend" (primary) and "Cancel" (secondary)
- "Save & Resend" calls `editMessage(index, newText)`
- "Cancel" reverts to static display
- Escape key also cancels

**Delete confirmation:**

When trash is clicked:
- Show a simple inline confirmation: "Delete this message and all messages after it?" with "Delete" (red) and "Cancel" buttons
- Or use `window.confirm()` for simplicity

**Disabled states:**
- Both icons hidden/disabled while `isStreaming` is true
- Both icons hidden on the very first user message if it's the only message (optional — deleting the only message is valid, it just clears the chat)

### Component structure:

```
MessageBubble (existing)
├── User message variant
│   ├── Static mode (default)
│   │   ├── Message text
│   │   └── Hover overlay: [pencil] [trash] icons
│   └── Edit mode (when editing)
│       ├── Textarea with current content
│       └── [Save & Resend] [Cancel] buttons
└── Assistant message variant (unchanged)
```

No new component files needed — all changes within `MessageBubble.tsx`.

## Section 5: Error Handling & Edge Cases

**Session file not found:**
- If the JSONL file doesn't exist for the given `session_id`, return HTTP 404 with clear error message
- Frontend shows error toast/message

**Invalid message index:**
- If `user_message_index` is out of range (no Nth user query found), return HTTP 400
- Frontend shouldn't allow this, but backend validates defensively

**Edit while streaming:**
- Frontend disables edit/delete icons during `isStreaming`
- Backend: if somehow called during an active stream, the JSONL file might be locked — return HTTP 409 (conflict)

**Concurrent edits:**
- Not a concern (single-user app), but JSONL write uses atomic read-then-write pattern

**Empty conversation after delete:**
- If user deletes the first message, all messages are cleared
- `sessionIdRef` is reset to null (next message starts a fresh session)
- Frontend shows empty chat state (same as initial state)

**JSONL path resolution:**
- The session path depends on the project CWD — we use the same CWD that the `ClaudeSDKClient` runs in
- If path encoding changes between SDK versions, truncation would fail — mitigated by reading the actual file listing if needed

**Partial truncation / crash during write:**
- Write truncated content to a temp file first, then atomically rename — prevents corruption on crash

{
  "file_path": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/docs/plans/2026-02-20-query-edit-delete-design.md",
  "content": "# Query Edit & Delete Design\n\n## Problem\n\nUsers cannot modify or delete their messages in the agent chat. If a user sends a poorly worded query or wants to refine their question, they must send a new message, creating noise in the conversation. There is no way to \"rewind\" the conversation to an earlier state.\n\n## Solution\n\nAllow users to **edit** or **delete** their natural language messages in the agent chat:\n\n- **Edit**: Replace a user message in-place, discard everything after it, and re-send the modified message for a fresh agent response\n- **Delete**: Remove a user message and everything after it, rewinding the conversation to just before that point\n\n## Architecture\n\n### Approach: JSONL Session Truncation + Resume\n\nThe Claude Agent SDK stores conversation state as JSONL files at `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/<session-id>.jsonl`. Each line is a JSON entry with types: `user`, `assistant`, `progress`, `system`, `file-history-snapshot`.\n\nUser query entries are identified by `type == \"user\"` with `message.content` as a string (tool result entries have array content).\n\nTo edit/delete a message:\n1. Read the session JSONL file\n2. Walk entries, counting user query entries to find the target index\n3. Truncate the file at that entry (remove it and everything after)\n4. For edit: resume the truncated session with the new message\n5. For delete: return success\n\nThis preserves exact agent state up to the truncation point, as the SDK reconstructs conversation from the JSONL on resume.\n\n## Data Flow\n\n### Edit Flow\n\n```\nUser hovers message -> clicks pencil icon -> message enters edit mode (textarea)\n  -> user modifies text -> clicks \"Save & Resend\"\n  -> Frontend: truncate messages array at edit index\n  -> POST /api/chat/edit { session_id, user_message_index, new_message }\n  -> Backend: truncate JSONL at Nth user query entry\n  -> Backend: resume session with edited message, stream SSE response\n  -> Frontend: receive SSE stream (same callbacks as normal chat)\n  -> New assistant response appears\n```\n\n### Delete Flow\n\n```\nUser hovers message -> clicks trash icon -> confirmation prompt\n  -> POST /api/chat/delete { session_id, user_message_index }\n  -> Backend: truncate JSONL at Nth user query entry\n  -> Backend: return { ok: true }\n  -> Frontend: truncate messages array at delete index\n```\n\n## Backend API\n\n### POST /api/chat/edit\n\nRequest:\n```python\nclass ChatEditRequest(BaseModel):\n    session_id: str          # Current session ID\n    user_message_index: int  # 0-based index of the user query to replace\n    new_message: str         # The edited message text\n```\n\nResponse: SSE stream (same format as `/api/chat`)\n\nLogic:\n1. Validate session_id exists as a JSONL file\n2. Call `truncate_session(session_id, user_message_index)`\n3. Call `stream_chat(new_message, session_id)` to resume and stream\n4. Return SSE streaming response\n\n### POST /api/chat/delete\n\nRequest:\n```python\nclass ChatDeleteRequest(BaseModel):\n    session_id: str          # Current session ID\n    user_message_index: int  # 0-based index of the user query to delete from\n```\n\nResponse: `{ \"ok\": true }`\n\nLogic:\n1. Validate session_id exists\n2. Call `truncate_session(session_id, user_message_index)`\n3. Return success\n\n### Session Truncation Utility (backend/app/session.py)\n\n```python\nimport json\nimport os\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\ndef truncate_session(session_id: str, user_message_index: int) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Truncate session JSONL file, removing the Nth user query and everything after.\"\"\"\n    session_path = _get_session_path(session_id)\n    if not session_path.exists():\n        raise FileNotFoundError(f\"Session file not found: {session_id}\")\n\n    with open(session_path, 'r') as f:\n        lines = f.readlines()\n\n    user_query_count = 0\n    truncate_at = len(lines)\n\n    for i, line in enumerate(lines):\n        entry = json.loads(line)\n        if _is_user_query(entry):\n            if user_query_count == user_message_index:\n                truncate_at = i\n                break\n            user_query_count += 1\n\n    if truncate_at == len(lines):\n        raise ValueError(f\"User message index {user_message_index} not found\")\n\n    # Atomic write: write to temp file then rename\n    dir_path = session_path.parent\n    with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(mode='w', dir=dir_path, delete=False, suffix='.jsonl') as tmp:\n        tmp.writelines(lines[:truncate_at])\n        tmp_path = tmp.name\n    os.replace(tmp_path, session_path)\n\ndef _is_user_query(entry: dict) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Check if a JSONL entry is a user query (not a tool result).\"\"\"\n    return (\n        entry.get(\"type\") == \"user\"\n        and isinstance(entry.get(\"message\", {}).get(\"content\"), str)\n    )\n\ndef _get_session_path(session_id: str) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Construct path to session JSONL file.\"\"\"\n    cwd = os.getcwd()\n    encoded = cwd.replace(\"/\", \"-\")\n    return Path.home() / \".claude\" / \"projects\" / encoded / f\"{session_id}.jsonl\"\n```\n\n### Route Handler (backend/app/routes/chat.py)\n\nAdd `chat_edit` and `chat_delete` handlers alongside existing `chat` handler.\n\n## Frontend Changes\n\n### AgentContext.tsx\n\nNew callbacks exposed via context:\n\n```typescript\neditMessage(messageIndex: number, newContent: string): void\ndeleteMessage(messageIndex: number): void\n```\n\n**editMessage logic:**\n1. Guard: if `isStreaming`, return\n2. Compute `user_message_index` by counting user messages in `messages.slice(0, messageIndex)`\n3. Truncate `messages` to `messages.slice(0, messageIndex)`\n4. Create new user message with edited content + empty assistant message\n5. Call `runAgentEditLoop` (posts to `/api/chat/edit`) with SSE callbacks\n6. Stream response using existing SSE callback infrastructure\n\n**deleteMessage logic:**\n1. Guard: if `isStreaming`, return\n2. Compute `user_message_index` by counting user messages in `messages.slice(0, messageIndex)`\n3. POST to `/api/chat/delete` with `{ session_id, user_message_index }`\n4. On success: truncate `messages` to `messages.slice(0, messageIndex)`\n5. If `messageIndex === 0`, clear `sessionIdRef` (no session left)\n\n### agentService.ts\n\nNew functions:\n\n```typescript\nfunction runAgentEditLoop(\n  sessionId: string,\n  userMessageIndex: number,\n  newMessage: string,\n  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,\n  signal: AbortSignal\n): void\n// Same SSE parsing as runAgentLoop, posts to /api/chat/edit\n\nasync function deleteAgentMessage(\n  sessionId: string,\n  userMessageIndex: number\n): Promise<void>\n// Simple fetch POST to /api/chat/delete\n```\n\n### MessageBubble.tsx\n\n**Hover actions on user messages:**\n- Show pencil (edit) and trash (delete) icon buttons on hover\n- Positioned at top-right of the user message bubble\n- Hidden when `isStreaming` is true\n\n**Edit mode:**\n- Pencil click transforms message into editable textarea\n- Pre-populated with current content, auto-focused\n- \"Save & Resend\" (primary) and \"Cancel\" (secondary) buttons below\n- Escape key cancels\n- Save calls `editMessage(index, newText)`\n\n**Delete confirmation:**\n- Trash click shows inline confirmation: \"Delete this and all following messages?\"\n- \"Delete\" (red) and \"Cancel\" buttons\n- Confirm calls `deleteMessage(index)`\n\n**Component structure:**\n```\nMessageBubble\n  User message\n    Static mode (default)\n      Message text\n      Hover overlay: [pencil] [trash]\n    Edit mode (when editing)\n      Textarea with current content\n      [Save & Resend] [Cancel]\n  Assistant message (unchanged)\n```\n\n## Error Handling\n\n| Scenario | Backend | Frontend |\n|----------|---------|----------|\n| Session file not found | HTTP 404 | Show error message |\n| Invalid message index | HTTP 400 | Should not happen (validated client-side) |\n| Edit while streaming | N/A | Icons disabled during streaming |\n| Concurrent request conflict | HTTP 409 | Show error message |\n| JSONL write failure | Atomic write prevents corruption | Show error, no state change |\n| Empty conversation after delete | N/A | Clear sessionIdRef, show empty chat |\n| First message deleted | N/A | Reset to initial state |\n\n## Files to Modify\n\n### New Files\n- `backend/app/session.py` — Session JSONL truncation utility\n\n### Modified Files\n- `backend/app/routes/chat.py` — Add edit/delete route handlers\n- `backend/app/main.py` — Register new routes (if separate router)\n- `frontend/src/agent/agentService.ts` — Add `runAgentEditLoop` and `deleteAgentMessage`\n- `frontend/src/AgentContext.tsx` — Add `editMessage` and `deleteMessage` callbacks\n- `frontend/src/components/MessageBubble.tsx` — Hover icons, edit mode, delete confirmation\n- `frontend/src/types.ts` — Add request/response types if needed\n"
}
```

     1→import json
     2→import logging
     3→from typing import AsyncIterator
     4→
     5→from claude_agent_sdk import (
     6→    ClaudeSDKClient,
     7→    ClaudeAgentOptions,
     8→    AssistantMessage,
     9→    UserMessage,
    10→    ResultMessage,
    11→    ToolUseBlock,
    12→    ToolResultBlock,
    13→)
    14→from claude_agent_sdk.types import StreamEvent
    15→from app.tools import create_duckdb_server
    16→from app.database import db
    17→from app.config import ANTHROPIC_MODEL
    18→from app.tracing import get_langfuse_client
    19→
    20→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    21→
    22→
    23→def build_system_prompt() -> str:
    24→    tables = db.list_tables()
    25→    prompt = """You are a helpful data analyst assistant working with a DuckDB database.
    26→You can execute SQL queries using the execute_sql tool to answer questions about the user's data.
    27→
    28→Guidelines:
    29→- Write clear, efficient DuckDB SQL queries
    30→- When exploring data, start with small queries (use LIMIT)
    31→- Explain your findings in plain language after getting results
    32→- If a query fails, try to fix it and retry
    33→- Use double quotes for table and column names that might conflict with reserved words
    34→"""
    35→    if not tables:
    36→        prompt += "\nNo tables are currently loaded. Ask the user to upload a CSV file first."
    37→    else:
    38→        prompt += "\nCurrently loaded tables:\n"
    39→        for table in tables:
    40→            prompt += f'\nTable: "{table["name"]}" ({table["rowCount"]} rows)\nColumns:\n'
    41→            for col in table["columns"]:
    42→                prompt += f'  - "{col["name"]}" ({col["type"]})\n'
    43→    return prompt
    44→
    45→
    46→def _extract_tool_result_text(content: object) -> str:
    47→    """Extract text from ToolResultBlock.content."""
    48→    if content is None:
    49→        return ""
    50→    if isinstance(content, str):
    51→        return content
    52→    if isinstance(content, list):
    53→        parts = []
    54→        for item in content:
    55→            if isinstance(item, dict) and item.get("type") == "text":
    56→                parts.append(item.get("text", ""))
    57→        return "\n".join(parts)
    58→    return str(content)
    59→
    60→
    61→async def stream_chat(message: str, session_id: str | None = None) -> AsyncIterator[str]:
    62→    """Stream agent chat responses as SSE events."""
    63→    duckdb_server = create_duckdb_server()
    64→
    65→    logger.info("Using model: %s", ANTHROPIC_MODEL)
    66→
    67→    # Use the --resume flag to continue an existing session
    68→    options = ClaudeAgentOptions(
    69→        model=ANTHROPIC_MODEL,
    70→        system_prompt=build_system_prompt(),
    71→        mcp_servers={"duckdb": duckdb_server},
    72→        allowed_tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
    73→        permission_mode="bypassPermissions",
    74→        max_turns=20,
    75→        include_partial_messages=True,
    76→        **({"resume": session_id} if session_id else {}),
    77→    )
    78→
    79→    client = ClaudeSDKClient(options=options)
    80→    # Will be set from the CLI's ResultMessage; use the passed-in value until then
    81→    actual_session_id = session_id
    82→
    83→    # --- Langfuse OTel tracing setup (conditional) ---
    84→    # Deferred: session_id is set after the CLI returns it in ResultMessage
    85→    langfuse = get_langfuse_client()
    86→    observation_ctx = None
    87→    propagate_ctx = None
    88→    if langfuse:
    89→        try:
    90→            observation_ctx = langfuse.start_as_current_observation(
    91→                name="agent-chat",
    92→                input={"message": message[:500]},
    93→                metadata={"model": ANTHROPIC_MODEL},
    94→            )
    95→            observation_ctx.__enter__()
    96→
    97→            # If resuming, propagate the known session_id for child spans
    98→            if session_id:
    99→                from langfuse import propagate_attributes
   100→                propagate_ctx = propagate_attributes(session_id=session_id)
   101→                propagate_ctx.__enter__()
   102→        except Exception as e:
   103→            logger.debug("Failed to set up Langfuse tracing context: %s", e)
   104→            observation_ctx = None
   105→
   106→    try:
   107→        await client.connect()
   108→        await client.query(message, session_id=session_id or "default")
   109→
   110→        current_text = ""
   111→        has_tool_calls = False
   112→        thinking_sent = False
   113→        has_thinking = False
   114→        sql_result_ids: set[str] = set()
   115→        tool_names: dict[str, str] = {}
   116→
   117→        async for msg in client.receive_response():
   118→            if isinstance(msg, StreamEvent):
   119→                event = msg.event
   120→                event_type = event.get("type", "")
   121→
   122→                if event_type == "content_block_delta":
   123→                    delta = event.get("delta", {})
   124→                    delta_type = delta.get("type", "")
   125→                    if delta_type == "thinking_delta":
   126→                        text = delta.get("thinking", "")
   127→                        if text:
   128→                            current_text += text
   129→                            yield f"event: thinking\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"
   130→                    elif delta_type == "text_delta":
   131→                        text = delta.get("text", "")
   132→                        current_text += text
   133→                        event_name = "thinking" if not has_tool_calls else "answer"
   134→                        yield f"event: {event_name}\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"
   135→
   136→                elif event_type == "content_block_start":
   137→                    block = event.get("content_block", {})
   138→                    block_type = block.get("type")
   139→                    if block_type == "thinking":
   140→                        has_thinking = True
   141→                    elif block_type == "text":
   142→                        if has_thinking:
   143→                            yield f"event: thinking_done\ndata: {json.dumps({})}\n\n"
   144→                    elif block_type == "tool_use":
   145→                        has_thinking = False
   146→                        if current_text.strip() and not thinking_sent:
   147→                            thinking_sent = True
   148→                        has_tool_calls = True
   149→
   150→            elif isinstance(msg, AssistantMessage):
   151→                for block in msg.content:
   152→                    if isinstance(block, ToolUseBlock):
   153→                        has_tool_calls = True
   154→                        tool_name = getattr(block, "name", "") or ""
   155→                        tool_names[block.id] = tool_name
   156→                        sql = block.input.get("sql", "")
   157→                        command = block.input.get("command", "")
   158→
   159→                        # Emit tool_call for ALL tool types
   160→                        tool_call_data: dict = {"id": block.id, "name": tool_name}
   161→                        if sql:
   162→                            tool_call_data["sql"] = sql
   163→                        if command:
   164→                            tool_call_data["command"] = command
   165→                        if not sql and not command:
   166→                            tool_call_data["input"] = block.input
   167→                        yield f"event: tool_call\ndata: {json.dumps(tool_call_data, default=str)}\n\n"
   168→
   169→                        # For SQL tools, execute query for structured results
   170→                        if sql:
   171→                            sql_result_ids.add(block.id)
   172→                            try:
   173→                                result = db.execute_query(sql)
   174→                                truncated = result["rows"][:100]
   175→                                yield f"event: tool_result\ndata: {json.dumps({'id': block.id, 'name': tool_name, 'sql': sql, 'columns': result['columns'], 'rows': truncated, 'rowCount': result['rowCount']}, default=str)}\n\n"
   176→                            except Exception as e:
   177→                                yield f"event: tool_result\ndata: {json.dumps({'id': block.id, 'name': tool_name, 'sql': sql, 'error': str(e)})}\n\n"
   178→
   179→            elif isinstance(msg, UserMessage):
   180→                # Capture tool results from the SDK for non-SQL tools
   181→                content = msg.content
   182→                if isinstance(content, list):
   183→                    for block in content:
   184→                        if isinstance(block, ToolResultBlock):
   185→                            if block.tool_use_id in sql_result_ids:
   186→                                continue
   187→                            output = _extract_tool_result_text(block.content)
   188→                            name = tool_names.get(block.tool_use_id, "")
   189→                            result_data: dict = {
   190→                                "id": block.tool_use_id,
   191→                                "name": name,
   192→                                "output": output,
   193→                            }
   194→                            if block.is_error:
   195→                                result_data["error"] = output
   196→                            yield f"event: tool_result\ndata: {json.dumps(result_data, default=str)}\n\n"
   197→
   198→            elif isinstance(msg, ResultMessage):
   199→                # Use the CLI's session_id so --resume can find it
   200→                actual_session_id = msg.session_id or actual_session_id
   201→                if msg.is_error and msg.result:
   202→                    yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': msg.result})}\n\n"
   203→                yield f"event: done\ndata: {json.dumps({'session_id': actual_session_id})}\n\n"
   204→
   205→    except Exception as e:
   206→        yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': str(e)})}\n\n"
   207→    finally:
   208→        # Update trace with the CLI's session_id so Langfuse session matches
   209→        if langfuse and observation_ctx:
   210→            try:
   211→                langfuse.update_current_trace(
   212→                    session_id=actual_session_id,
   213→                    output={"session_id": actual_session_id},
   214→                )
   215→                if propagate_ctx:
   216→                    propagate_ctx.__exit__(None, None, None)
   217→                observation_ctx.__exit__(None, None, None)
   218→            except Exception as e:
   219→                logger.debug("Failed to finalize Langfuse trace: %s", e)
   220→            try:
   221→                langfuse.flush()
   222→            except Exception as e:
   223→                logger.debug("Failed to flush Langfuse: %s", e)
   224→
   225→        try:
   226→            await client.disconnect()
   227→        except Exception:
   228→            pass
   229→

     1→from pathlib import Path
     2→
     3→from fastapi import FastAPI
     4→from fastapi.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware
     5→from fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles
     6→from fastapi.responses import FileResponse
     7→
     8→from app.routes import tables, query, chat, langfuse_status
     9→
    10→app = FastAPI(title="DuckDB Data Agent API")
    11→
    12→app.add_middleware(
    13→    CORSMiddleware,
    14→    allow_origins=["*"],
    15→    allow_credentials=True,
    16→    allow_methods=["*"],
    17→    allow_headers=["*"],
    18→)
    19→
    20→app.include_router(tables.router)
    21→app.include_router(query.router)
    22→app.include_router(chat.router)
    23→app.include_router(langfuse_status.router)
    24→
    25→
    26→@app.get("/api/health")
    27→async def health():
    28→    return {"status": "ok"}
    29→
    30→
    31→# Serve frontend static files in production
    32→STATIC_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "static"
    33→
    34→if STATIC_DIR.is_dir():
    35→    app.mount("/assets", StaticFiles(directory=STATIC_DIR / "assets"), name="static-assets")
    36→
    37→    @app.get("/{full_path:path}")
    38→    async def serve_frontend(full_path: str):
    39→        """Serve frontend for any non-API route (SPA fallback)."""
    40→        file_path = STATIC_DIR / full_path
    41→        if full_path and file_path.is_file():
    42→            return FileResponse(file_path)
    43→        return FileResponse(STATIC_DIR / "index.html")
    44→

     1→import type { ToolCallResult } from '../types';
     2→
     3→interface AgentCallbacks {
     4→  onTextChunk: (text: string) => void;
     5→  onThinkingDone: () => void;
     6→  onToolCall: (pending: ToolCallResult) => void;
     7→  onToolResult: (result: ToolCallResult) => void;
     8→  onDone: (sessionId: string | null) => void;
     9→  onError: (error: string) => void;
    10→}
    11→
    12→export type { AgentCallbacks };
    13→
    14→export async function runAgentLoop(
    15→  message: string,
    16→  sessionId: string | null,
    17→  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
    18→  signal?: AbortSignal,
    19→): Promise<void> {
    20→  try {
    21→    const response = await fetch('/api/chat', {
    22→      method: 'POST',
    23→      headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
    24→      body: JSON.stringify({ message, session_id: sessionId }),
    25→      signal,
    26→    });
    27→
    28→    if (!response.ok) {
    29→      const errorText = await response.text();
    30→      callbacks.onError(`Server error: ${errorText}`);
    31→      return;
    32→    }
    33→
    34→    const reader = response.body?.getReader();
    35→    if (!reader) {
    36→      callbacks.onError('No response stream');
    37→      return;
    38→    }
    39→
    40→    const decoder = new TextDecoder();
    41→    let buffer = '';
    42→
    43→    while (true) {
    44→      const { done, value } = await reader.read();
    45→      if (done) break;
    46→
    47→      buffer += decoder.decode(value, { stream: true });
    48→      const lines = buffer.split('\n');
    49→      buffer = lines.pop() ?? '';
    50→
    51→      let eventType = '';
    52→      for (const line of lines) {
    53→        if (line.startsWith('event: ')) {
    54→          eventType = line.slice(7).trim();
    55→        } else if (line.startsWith('data: ')) {
    56→          const data = line.slice(6);
    57→          try {
    58→            const parsed = JSON.parse(data);
    59→            handleSSEEvent(eventType, parsed, callbacks);
    60→          } catch {
    61→            // Skip malformed JSON
    62→          }
    63→          eventType = '';
    64→        }
    65→      }
    66→    }
    67→  } catch (e: unknown) {
    68→    if (signal?.aborted) return;
    69→    const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Connection failed';
    70→    callbacks.onError(msg);
    71→  }
    72→}
    73→
    74→function handleSSEEvent(
    75→  eventType: string,
    76→  data: Record<string, unknown>,
    77→  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
    78→) {
    79→  switch (eventType) {
    80→    case 'thinking':
    81→    case 'answer':
    82→      callbacks.onTextChunk(data.text as string);
    83→      break;
    84→    case 'thinking_done':
    85→      callbacks.onThinkingDone();
    86→      break;
    87→    case 'tool_call':
    88→      callbacks.onToolCall({
    89→        toolCallId: (data.id as string) ?? '',
    90→        toolName: (data.name as string) ?? undefined,
    91→        sql: (data.sql as string) ?? '',
    92→        command: (data.command as string) ?? undefined,
    93→        toolInput: (data.input as Record<string, unknown>) ?? undefined,
    94→        columns: [],
    95→        rows: [],
    96→        rowCount: 0,
    97→      });
    98→      break;
    99→    case 'tool_result': {
   100→      const result: ToolCallResult = {
   101→        toolCallId: (data.id as string) ?? '',
   102→        toolName: (data.name as string) ?? undefined,
   103→        sql: (data.sql as string) ?? '',
   104→        columns: (data.columns as string[]) ?? [],
   105→        rows: (data.rows as Record<string, unknown>[]) ?? [],
   106→        rowCount: (data.rowCount as number) ?? 0,
   107→        error: (data.error as string) ?? undefined,
   108→        output: (data.output as string) ?? undefined,
   109→        rawContent: (data.content as string) ?? undefined,
   110→      };
   111→      callbacks.onToolResult(result);
   112→      break;
   113→    }
   114→    case 'done':
   115→      callbacks.onDone((data.session_id as string) ?? null);
   116→      break;
   117→    case 'error':
   118→      callbacks.onError((data.message as string) ?? 'Unknown error');
   119→      break;
   120→  }
   121→}
   122→
```
