     1→import {
     2→  createContext,
     3→  useCallback,
     4→  useContext,
     5→  useRef,
     6→  useState,
     7→  type ReactNode,
     8→} from 'react';
     9→import { runAgentLoop } from './agent/agentService';
    10→import type { ChatMessage, ContentSegment, TableInfo, ToolCallResult } from './types';
    11→
    12→interface AgentContextValue {
    13→  messages: ChatMessage[];
    14→  isStreaming: boolean;
    15→  sendMessage: (text: string) => void;
    16→  clearMessages: () => void;
    17→}
    18→
    19→const AgentContext = createContext<AgentContextValue>({
    20→  messages: [],
    21→  isStreaming: false,
    22→  sendMessage: () => {},
    23→  clearMessages: () => {},
    24→});
    25→
    26→function generateId() {
    27→  return Math.random().toString(36).slice(2, 10);
    28→}
    29→
    30→export function AgentProvider({
    31→  children,
    32→  refreshTables,
    33→}: {
    34→  children: ReactNode;
    35→  tables: TableInfo[];
    36→  refreshTables: () => Promise<void>;
    37→}) {
    38→  const [messages, setMessages] = useState<ChatMessage[]>([]);
    39→  const [isStreaming, setIsStreaming] = useState(false);
    40→  const abortRef = useRef<AbortController | null>(null);
    41→  const textBufferRef = useRef('');
    42→  const flushTimerRef = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null>(null);
    43→  const assistantIdRef = useRef('');
    44→  const segmentsRef = useRef<ContentSegment[]>([]);
    45→  const currentTextRef = useRef('');
    46→  const sessionIdRef = useRef<string | null>(null);
    47→
    48→  const flushText = useCallback(() => {
    49→    const text = textBufferRef.current;
    50→    if (!text) return;
    51→    const id = assistantIdRef.current;
    52→    currentTextRef.current += text;
    53→    setMessages((prev) =>
    54→      prev.map((m) =>
    55→        m.id === id ? { ...m, content: m.content + text } : m
    56→      )
    57→    );
    58→    textBufferRef.current = '';
    59→  }, []);
    60→
    61→  const sendMessage = useCallback(
    62→    async (text: string) => {
    63→      if (isStreaming) return;
    64→
    65→      const userMsg: ChatMessage = {
    66→        id: generateId(),
    67→        role: 'user',
    68→        content: text,
    69→      };
    70→
    71→      const assistantId = generateId();
    72→      assistantIdRef.current = assistantId;
    73→      const assistantMsg: ChatMessage = {
    74→        id: assistantId,
    75→        role: 'assistant',
    76→        content: '',
    77→        toolCalls: [],
    78→        isStreaming: true,
    79→      };
    80→
    81→      setMessages((prev) => [...prev, userMsg, assistantMsg]);
    82→      setIsStreaming(true);
    83→      textBufferRef.current = '';
    84→      segmentsRef.current = [];
    85→      currentTextRef.current = '';
    86→
    87→      const controller = new AbortController();
    88→      abortRef.current = controller;
    89→
    90→      await runAgentLoop(
    91→        text,
    92→        sessionIdRef.current,
    93→        {
    94→          onTextChunk: (chunk) => {
    95→            textBufferRef.current += chunk;
    96→            if (!flushTimerRef.current) {
    97→              flushTimerRef.current = setTimeout(() => {
    98→                flushText();
    99→                flushTimerRef.current = null;
   100→              }, 50);
   101→            }
   102→          },
   103→          onThinkingDone: () => {
   104→            // Extended thinking just ended and a text block is starting.
   105→            // Create a thinking segment from accumulated thinking text.
   106→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {
   107→              clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
   108→              flushTimerRef.current = null;
   109→            }
   110→            flushText();
   111→            if (currentTextRef.current.trim()) {
   112→              segmentsRef.current.push({ type: 'thinking', text: currentTextRef.current });
   113→              currentTextRef.current = '';
   114→            }
   115→            setMessages((prev) =>
   116→              prev.map((m) =>
   117→                m.id === assistantId
   118→                  ? { ...m, currentPhase: 'answer', segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   119→                  : m
   120→              )
   121→            );
   122→          },
   123→          onToolCall: (pending: ToolCallResult) => {
   124→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {
   125→              clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
   126→              flushTimerRef.current = null;
   127→            }
   128→            flushText();
   129→            if (currentTextRef.current.trim()) {
   130→              segmentsRef.current.push({ type: 'thinking', text: currentTextRef.current });
   131→              currentTextRef.current = '';
   132→            }
   133→            // Add a pending tool segment so the input is shown immediately
   134→            segmentsRef.current.push({
   135→              type: 'tool',
   136→              toolResult: pending,
   137→            });
   138→            setMessages((prev) =>
   139→              prev.map((m) =>
   140→                m.id === assistantId
   141→                  ? { ...m, segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   142→                  : m
   143→              )
   144→            );
   145→          },
   146→          onToolResult: (result: ToolCallResult) => {
   147→            // Merge result into pending tool segment (keep input info, add output)
   148→            const pendingIdx = segmentsRef.current.findIndex(
   149→              (s) => s.type === 'tool' && s.toolResult?.toolCallId === result.toolCallId
   150→            );
   151→            if (pendingIdx !== -1) {
   152→              const pending = segmentsRef.current[pendingIdx].toolResult!;
   153→              segmentsRef.current[pendingIdx] = {
   154→                type: 'tool',
   155→                toolResult: {
   156→                  ...pending,
   157→                  ...result,
   158→                  toolName: result.toolName || pending.toolName,
   159→                  command: result.command || pending.command,
   160→                  toolInput: result.toolInput || pending.toolInput,
   161→                },
   162→              };
   163→            } else {
   164→              segmentsRef.current.push({ type: 'tool', toolResult: result });
   165→            }
   166→            setMessages((prev) =>
   167→              prev.map((m) =>
   168→                m.id === assistantId
   169→                  ? { ...m, toolCalls: [...(m.toolCalls || []), result], segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   170→                  : m
   171→              )
   172→            );
   173→            refreshTables();
   174→          },
   175→          onDone: (newSessionId) => {
   176→            if (newSessionId) sessionIdRef.current = newSessionId;
   177→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {
   178→              clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
   179→              flushTimerRef.current = null;
   180→            }
   181→            flushText();
   182→            if (currentTextRef.current.trim()) {
   183→              segmentsRef.current.push({
   184→                type: 'answer',
   185→                text: currentTextRef.current,
   186→              });
   187→              currentTextRef.current = '';
   188→            }
   189→            setMessages((prev) =>
   190→              prev.map((m) =>
   191→                m.id === assistantId
   192→                  ? { ...m, isStreaming: false, currentPhase: undefined, segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   193→                  : m
   194→              )
   195→            );
   196→            setIsStreaming(false);
   197→            abortRef.current = null;
   198→          },
   199→          onError: (error) => {
   200→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {
   201→              clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
   202→              flushTimerRef.current = null;
   203→            }
   204→            flushText();
   205→            setMessages((prev) =>
   206→              prev.map((m) =>
   207→                m.id === assistantId
   208→                  ? { ...m, content: m.content + `\n\n**Error:** ${error}`, isStreaming: false }
   209→                  : m
   210→              )
   211→            );
   212→            setIsStreaming(false);
   213→            abortRef.current = null;
   214→          },
   215→        },
   216→        controller.signal
   217→      );
   218→    },
   219→    [isStreaming, messages, flushText, refreshTables]
   220→  );
   221→
   222→  const clearMessages = useCallback(() => {
   223→    if (abortRef.current) {
   224→      abortRef.current.abort();
   225→    }
   226→    setMessages([]);
   227→    setIsStreaming(false);
   228→    sessionIdRef.current = null;
   229→  }, []);
   230→
   231→  return (
   232→    <AgentContext.Provider
   233→      value={{ messages, isStreaming, sendMessage, clearMessages }}
   234→    >
   235→      {children}
   236→    </AgentContext.Provider>
   237→  );
   238→}
   239→
   240→export function useAgent() {
   241→  return useContext(AgentContext);
   242→}
   243→
```

     1→import ReactMarkdown from 'react-markdown';
     2→import remarkGfm from 'remark-gfm';
     3→import type { ChatMessage, ContentSegment } from '../types';
     4→import { InlineQueryResult } from './InlineQueryResult';
     5→import './MessageBubble.css';
     6→
     7→function getLastThinkingLine(segments: ContentSegment[], streamingRemainder?: string): string {
     8→  // Use streaming remainder if available
     9→  if (streamingRemainder?.trim()) {
    10→    const lines = streamingRemainder.trim().split('\n').filter((l) => l.trim());
    11→    const last = lines[lines.length - 1] || '';
    12→    return last.length > 100 ? last.slice(0, 100) + '...' : last;
    13→  }
    14→  // Otherwise use last thinking segment's last line
    15→  for (let i = segments.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    16→    if (segments[i].type === 'thinking' && segments[i].text?.trim()) {
    17→      const lines = segments[i].text!.trim().split('\n').filter((l) => l.trim());
    18→      const last = lines[lines.length - 1] || '';
    19→      return last.length > 100 ? last.slice(0, 100) + '...' : last;
    20→    }
    21→  }
    22→  return 'Thinking...';
    23→}
    24→
    25→function ThinkingBlock({ segments, streamingRemainder, isActivelyStreaming }: {
    26→  segments: ContentSegment[];
    27→  streamingRemainder?: string;
    28→  isActivelyStreaming: boolean;
    29→}) {
    30→  // All non-answer segments go inside the thinking block
    31→  const thinkingSegments = segments.filter((s) => s.type !== 'answer');
    32→  const hasContent = thinkingSegments.some(
    33→    (s) => (s.type === 'thinking' && s.text?.trim()) || (s.type === 'tool' && s.toolResult)
    34→  ) || streamingRemainder?.trim();
    35→
    36→  if (!hasContent) return null;
    37→
    38→  const summary = getLastThinkingLine(segments, streamingRemainder);
    39→
    40→  return (
    41→    <details className="message-bubble__segment message-bubble__segment--thinking message-bubble__collapsible" open={isActivelyStreaming || undefined}>
    42→      <summary className="message-bubble__collapsible-summary">
    43→        <span className="message-bubble__segment-label">Thinking</span>
    44→        <span className="message-bubble__collapsible-preview">{summary}</span>
    45→      </summary>
    46→      <div className="message-bubble__thinking-body">
    47→        {thinkingSegments.map((seg, i) => {
    48→          if (seg.type === 'thinking' && seg.text?.trim()) {
    49→            return (
    50→              <div key={i} className="message-bubble__segment-content">
    51→                <ReactMarkdown remarkPlugins={[remarkGfm]}>{seg.text}</ReactMarkdown>
    52→              </div>
    53→            );
    54→          }
    55→          if (seg.type === 'tool' && seg.toolResult) {
    56→            return (
    57→              <div key={i} className="message-bubble__tool-segment">
    58→                <InlineQueryResult result={seg.toolResult} />
    59→              </div>
    60→            );
    61→          }
    62→          return null;
    63→        })}
    64→        {streamingRemainder?.trim() && (
    65→          <div className="message-bubble__segment-content">
    66→            <ReactMarkdown remarkPlugins={[remarkGfm]}>{streamingRemainder}</ReactMarkdown>
    67→          </div>
    68→        )}
    69→      </div>
    70→    </details>
    71→  );
    72→}
    73→
    74→export function MessageBubble({ message }: { message: ChatMessage }) {
    75→  const isUser = message.role === 'user';
    76→  const hasSegments = !isUser && message.segments && message.segments.length > 0;
    77→
    78→  // Compute streaming remainder text
    79→  let streamingRemainder: string | undefined;
    80→  if (hasSegments && message.isStreaming && message.content) {
    81→    const segmentedText = message.segments!
    82→      .filter((s) => s.type !== 'tool')
    83→      .map((s) => s.text || '')
    84→      .join('');
    85→    const remaining = message.content.slice(segmentedText.length);
    86→    if (remaining.trim()) {
    87→      streamingRemainder = remaining;
    88→    }
    89→  }
    90→
    91→  // Check if currently in a thinking-only phase (no answer content yet)
    92→  const hasAnswer = hasSegments && message.segments!.some((s) => s.type === 'answer');
    93→  const isInAnswerPhase = message.currentPhase === 'answer';
    94→  const isThinkingPhase = !!message.isStreaming && !hasAnswer && !isInAnswerPhase;
    95→
    96→  // Extract answer segments
    97→  const answerSegments = hasSegments
    98→    ? message.segments!.filter((s) => s.type === 'answer' && s.text?.trim())
    99→    : [];
   100→
   101→  return (
   102→    <div className={`message-bubble message-bubble--${message.role}`}>
   103→      <div className="message-bubble__header">
   104→        {isUser ? 'You' : 'Assistant'}
   105→      </div>
   106→
   107→      {hasSegments ? (
   108→        <div className="message-bubble__segments">
   109→          <ThinkingBlock
   110→            segments={message.segments!}
   111→            streamingRemainder={isThinkingPhase ? streamingRemainder : undefined}
   112→            isActivelyStreaming={isThinkingPhase}
   113→          />
   114→          {answerSegments.map((seg, i) => (
   115→            <div key={i} className="message-bubble__segment message-bubble__segment--answer">
   116→              <div className="message-bubble__segment-label message-bubble__segment-label--answer">Answer</div>
   117→              <div className="message-bubble__segment-content">
   118→                <ReactMarkdown remarkPlugins={[remarkGfm]}>{seg.text!}</ReactMarkdown>
   119→              </div>
   120→            </div>
   121→          ))}
   122→          {isInAnswerPhase && !hasAnswer && streamingRemainder?.trim() && (
   123→            <div className="message-bubble__segment message-bubble__segment--answer">
   124→              <div className="message-bubble__segment-label message-bubble__segment-label--answer">Answer</div>
   125→              <div className="message-bubble__segment-content">
   126→                <ReactMarkdown remarkPlugins={[remarkGfm]}>{streamingRemainder}</ReactMarkdown>
   127→              </div>
   128→            </div>
   129→          )}
   130→          {message.isStreaming && !message.content && (
   131→            <span className="message-bubble__typing">Thinking...</span>
   132→          )}
   133→        </div>
   134→      ) : (
   135→        <>
   136→          <div className="message-bubble__content">
   137→            {message.content ? (
   138→              <ReactMarkdown remarkPlugins={[remarkGfm]}>{message.content}</ReactMarkdown>
   139→            ) : message.isStreaming ? (
   140→              <span className="message-bubble__typing">Thinking...</span>
   141→            ) : null}
   142→          </div>
   143→          {message.toolCalls && message.toolCalls.length > 0 && (
   144→            <div className="message-bubble__tools">
   145→              {message.toolCalls.map((tc) => (
   146→                <InlineQueryResult key={tc.toolCallId} result={tc} />
   147→              ))}
   148→            </div>
   149→          )}
   150→        </>
   151→      )}
   152→    </div>
   153→  );
   154→}
   155→

     1→export interface ColumnInfo {
     2→  name: string;
     3→  type: string;
     4→}
     5→
     6→export interface TableInfo {
     7→  name: string;
     8→  columns: ColumnInfo[];
     9→  rowCount: number;
    10→}
    11→

     1→import { useEffect, useRef } from 'react';
     2→import { useAgent } from '../AgentContext';
     3→import { ChatInput } from './ChatInput';
     4→import { MessageBubble } from './MessageBubble';
     5→import type { LangfuseStatus } from '../types';
     6→import './AgentPanel.css';
     7→
     8→interface AgentPanelProps {
     9→  langfuseStatus: LangfuseStatus;
    10→}
    11→
    12→export function AgentPanel({ langfuseStatus }: AgentPanelProps) {
    13→  const { messages, clearMessages } = useAgent();
    14→  const bottomRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
    15→
    16→  useEffect(() => {
    17→    bottomRef.current?.scrollIntoView({ behavior: 'smooth' });
    18→  }, [messages]);
    19→
    20→  return (
    21→    <div className="agent-panel">
    22→      <div className="agent-panel__header">
    23→        <span className="agent-panel__title">Agent Mode</span>
    24→        <div className="agent-panel__actions">
    25→          <button
    26→            className={`agent-panel__langfuse ${!langfuseStatus.enabled ? 'agent-panel__langfuse--disabled' : ''}`}
    27→            disabled={!langfuseStatus.enabled}
    28→            title={langfuseStatus.enabled ? 'Open Langfuse dashboard' : 'Langfuse not configured'}
    29→            onClick={() => {
    30→              if (langfuseStatus.dashboardUrl) {
    31→                window.open(langfuseStatus.dashboardUrl, '_blank', 'noopener,noreferrer');
    32→              }
    33→            }}
    34→          >
    35→            <img src="/langfuse-color.svg" alt="Langfuse" className="agent-panel__langfuse-icon" />
    36→            Langfuse Traces
    37→          </button>
    38→          {messages.length > 0 && (
    39→            <button className="agent-panel__clear" onClick={clearMessages}>
    40→              Clear
    41→            </button>
    42→          )}
    43→        </div>
    44→      </div>
    45→      <div className="agent-panel__messages">
    46→        {messages.length === 0 && (
    47→          <div className="agent-panel__empty">
    48→            Ask a question about your data, and the agent will write and run SQL queries to find the answer.
    49→          </div>
    50→        )}
    51→        {messages.map((msg) => (
    52→          <MessageBubble key={msg.id} message={msg} />
    53→        ))}
    54→        <div ref={bottomRef} />
    55→      </div>
    56→      <ChatInput />
    57→    </div>
    58→  );
    59→}
    60→

```
     1→# Query Edit & Delete Design
     2→
     3→## Problem
     4→
     5→Users cannot modify or delete their messages in the agent chat. If a user sends a poorly worded query or wants to refine their question, they must send a new message, creating noise in the conversation. There is no way to "rewind" the conversation to an earlier state.
     6→
     7→## Solution
     8→
     9→Allow users to **edit** or **delete** their natural language messages in the agent chat:
    10→
    11→- **Edit**: Replace a user message in-place, discard everything after it, and re-send the modified message for a fresh agent response
    12→- **Delete**: Remove a user message and everything after it, rewinding the conversation to just before that point
    13→
    14→## Architecture
    15→
    16→### Approach: JSONL Session Truncation + Resume
    17→
    18→The Claude Agent SDK stores conversation state as JSONL files at `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/<session-id>.jsonl`. Each line is a JSON entry with types: `user`, `assistant`, `progress`, `system`, `file-history-snapshot`.
    19→
    20→User query entries are identified by `type == "user"` with `message.content` as a string (tool result entries have array content).
    21→
    22→To edit/delete a message:
    23→1. Read the session JSONL file
    24→2. Walk entries, counting user query entries to find the target index
    25→3. Truncate the file at that entry (remove it and everything after)
    26→4. For edit: resume the truncated session with the new message
    27→5. For delete: return success
    28→
    29→This preserves exact agent state up to the truncation point, as the SDK reconstructs conversation from the JSONL on resume.
    30→
    31→## Data Flow
    32→
    33→### Edit Flow
    34→
    35→```
    36→User hovers message -> clicks pencil icon -> message enters edit mode (textarea)
    37→  -> user modifies text -> clicks "Save & Resend"
    38→  -> Frontend: truncate messages array at edit index
    39→  -> POST /api/chat/edit { session_id, user_message_index, new_message }
    40→  -> Backend: truncate JSONL at Nth user query entry
    41→  -> Backend: resume session with edited message, stream SSE response
    42→  -> Frontend: receive SSE stream (same callbacks as normal chat)
    43→  -> New assistant response appears
    44→```
    45→
    46→### Delete Flow
    47→
    48→```
    49→User hovers message -> clicks trash icon -> confirmation prompt
    50→  -> POST /api/chat/delete { session_id, user_message_index }
    51→  -> Backend: truncate JSONL at Nth user query entry
    52→  -> Backend: return { ok: true }
    53→  -> Frontend: truncate messages array at delete index
    54→```
    55→
    56→## Backend API
    57→
    58→### POST /api/chat/edit
    59→
    60→Request:
    61→```python
    62→class ChatEditRequest(BaseModel):
    63→    session_id: str          # Current session ID
    64→    user_message_index: int  # 0-based index of the user query to replace
    65→    new_message: str         # The edited message text
    66→```
    67→
    68→Response: SSE stream (same format as `/api/chat`)
    69→
    70→Logic:
    71→1. Validate session_id exists as a JSONL file
    72→2. Call `truncate_session(session_id, user_message_index)`
    73→3. Call `stream_chat(new_message, session_id)` to resume and stream
    74→4. Return SSE streaming response
    75→
    76→### POST /api/chat/delete
    77→
    78→Request:
    79→```python
    80→class ChatDeleteRequest(BaseModel):
    81→    session_id: str          # Current session ID
    82→    user_message_index: int  # 0-based index of the user query to delete from
    83→```
    84→
    85→Response: `{ "ok": true }`
    86→
    87→Logic:
    88→1. Validate session_id exists
    89→2. Call `truncate_session(session_id, user_message_index)`
    90→3. Return success
    91→
    92→### Session Truncation Utility (backend/app/session.py)
    93→
    94→```python
    95→import json
    96→import os
    97→import tempfile
    98→from pathlib import Path
    99→
   100→def truncate_session(session_id: str, user_message_index: int) -> None:
   101→    """Truncate session JSONL file, removing the Nth user query and everything after."""
   102→    session_path = _get_session_path(session_id)
   103→    if not session_path.exists():
   104→        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Session file not found: {session_id}")
   105→
   106→    with open(session_path, 'r') as f:
   107→        lines = f.readlines()
   108→
   109→    user_query_count = 0
   110→    truncate_at = len(lines)
   111→
   112→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   113→        entry = json.loads(line)
   114→        if _is_user_query(entry):
   115→            if user_query_count == user_message_index:
   116→                truncate_at = i
   117→                break
   118→            user_query_count += 1
   119→
   120→    if truncate_at == len(lines):
   121→        raise ValueError(f"User message index {user_message_index} not found")
   122→
   123→    # Atomic write: write to temp file then rename
   124→    dir_path = session_path.parent
   125→    with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(mode='w', dir=dir_path, delete=False, suffix='.jsonl') as tmp:
   126→        tmp.writelines(lines[:truncate_at])
   127→        tmp_path = tmp.name
   128→    os.replace(tmp_path, session_path)
   129→
   130→def _is_user_query(entry: dict) -> bool:
   131→    """Check if a JSONL entry is a user query (not a tool result)."""
   132→    return (
   133→        entry.get("type") == "user"
   134→        and isinstance(entry.get("message", {}).get("content"), str)
   135→    )
   136→
   137→def _get_session_path(session_id: str) -> Path:
   138→    """Construct path to session JSONL file."""
   139→    cwd = os.getcwd()
   140→    encoded = cwd.replace("/", "-")
   141→    return Path.home() / ".claude" / "projects" / encoded / f"{session_id}.jsonl"
   142→```
   143→
   144→### Route Handler (backend/app/routes/chat.py)
   145→
   146→Add `chat_edit` and `chat_delete` handlers alongside existing `chat` handler.
   147→
   148→## Frontend Changes
   149→
   150→### AgentContext.tsx
   151→
   152→New callbacks exposed via context:
   153→
   154→```typescript
   155→editMessage(messageIndex: number, newContent: string): void
   156→deleteMessage(messageIndex: number): void
   157→```
   158→
   159→**editMessage logic:**
   160→1. Guard: if `isStreaming`, return
   161→2. Compute `user_message_index` by counting user messages in `messages.slice(0, messageIndex)`
   162→3. Truncate `messages` to `messages.slice(0, messageIndex)`
   163→4. Create new user message with edited content + empty assistant message
   164→5. Call `runAgentEditLoop` (posts to `/api/chat/edit`) with SSE callbacks
   165→6. Stream response using existing SSE callback infrastructure
   166→
   167→**deleteMessage logic:**
   168→1. Guard: if `isStreaming`, return
   169→2. Compute `user_message_index` by counting user messages in `messages.slice(0, messageIndex)`
   170→3. POST to `/api/chat/delete` with `{ session_id, user_message_index }`
   171→4. On success: truncate `messages` to `messages.slice(0, messageIndex)`
   172→5. If `messageIndex === 0`, clear `sessionIdRef` (no session left)
   173→
   174→### agentService.ts
   175→
   176→New functions:
   177→
   178→```typescript
   179→function runAgentEditLoop(
   180→  sessionId: string,
   181→  userMessageIndex: number,
   182→  newMessage: string,
   183→  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
   184→  signal: AbortSignal
   185→): void
   186→// Same SSE parsing as runAgentLoop, posts to /api/chat/edit
   187→
   188→async function deleteAgentMessage(
   189→  sessionId: string,
   190→  userMessageIndex: number
   191→): Promise<void>
   192→// Simple fetch POST to /api/chat/delete
   193→```
   194→
   195→### MessageBubble.tsx
   196→
   197→**Hover actions on user messages:**
   198→- Show pencil (edit) and trash (delete) icon buttons on hover
   199→- Positioned at top-right of the user message bubble
   200→- Hidden when `isStreaming` is true
   201→
   202→**Edit mode:**
   203→- Pencil click transforms message into editable textarea
   204→- Pre-populated with current content, auto-focused
   205→- "Save & Resend" (primary) and "Cancel" (secondary) buttons below
   206→- Escape key cancels
   207→- Save calls `editMessage(index, newText)`
   208→
   209→**Delete confirmation:**
   210→- Trash click shows inline confirmation: "Delete this and all following messages?"
   211→- "Delete" (red) and "Cancel" buttons
   212→- Confirm calls `deleteMessage(index)`
   213→
   214→**Component structure:**
   215→```
   216→MessageBubble
   217→  User message
   218→    Static mode (default)
   219→      Message text
   220→      Hover overlay: [pencil] [trash]
   221→    Edit mode (when editing)
   222→      Textarea with current content
   223→      [Save & Resend] [Cancel]
   224→  Assistant message (unchanged)
   225→```
   226→
   227→## Error Handling
   228→
   229→| Scenario | Backend | Frontend |
   230→|----------|---------|----------|
   231→| Session file not found | HTTP 404 | Show error message |
   232→| Invalid message index | HTTP 400 | Should not happen (validated client-side) |
   233→| Edit while streaming | N/A | Icons disabled during streaming |
   234→| Concurrent request conflict | HTTP 409 | Show error message |
   235→| JSONL write failure | Atomic write prevents corruption | Show error, no state change |
   236→| Empty conversation after delete | N/A | Clear sessionIdRef, show empty chat |
   237→| First message deleted | N/A | Reset to initial state |
   238→

   242→- `backend/app/session.py` — Session JSONL truncation utility

   244→### Modified Files

   246→- `backend/app/main.py` — Register new routes (if separate router)
   247→- `frontend/src/agent/agentService.ts` — Add `runAgentEditLoop` and `deleteAgentMessage`
   248→- `frontend/src/AgentContext.tsx` — Add `editMessage` and `deleteMessage` callbacks
   249→- `frontend/src/components/MessageBubble.tsx` — Hover icons, edit mode, delete confirmation
   250→- `frontend/src/types.ts` — Add request/response types if needed
   251→

setIsEditing(false);\n    setEditText(message.content);\n  };\n\n  const handleSaveEdit = () => {\n    const trimmed = editText.trim();\n    if (!trimmed || trimmed === message.content) {\n message.content.slice(segmentedText.length);\n    if (remaining.trim()) {\n      streamingRemainder = remaining;\n    }\n  }\n\n  const hasAnswer = hasSegments && message.segments!.some((s) => s.type hasSegments\n    ? message.segments!.filter((s) => s.type === 'answer' && s.text?.trim())\n    : [];\n\n  return (\n    <div className={`message-bubble message-bubble--${message.role}`}>\n      <div (\n        <div className=\"message-bubble__segments\">\n          <ThinkingBlock\n            segments={message.segments!}\n            streamingRemainder={isThinkingPhase ? streamingRemainder : undefined}\n            isActivelyStreaming={isThinkingPhase}\n          />\n          {answerSegments.map((seg, i) => (\n            <div key={i} className=\"message-bubble__segment message-bubble__segment--answer\">\n              <div className=\"message-bubble__segment-label message-bubble__segment-label--answer\">Answer</div>\n              <div className=\"message-bubble__segment-content\">\n                <ReactMarkdown remarkPlugins={[remarkGfm]}>{seg.text!}</ReactMarkdown>\n              </div>\n            </div>\n          ))}\n          {isInAnswerPhase && !hasAnswer && streamingRemainder?.trim() && (\n            <div className=\"message-bubble__segment message-bubble__segment--answer\">\n              <div className=\"message-bubble__segment-label message-bubble__segment-label--answer\">Answer</div>\n              <div className=\"message-bubble__segment-content\">\n                <ReactMarkdown remarkPlugins={[remarkGfm]}>{streamingRemainder}</ReactMarkdown>\n              </div>\n            </div>\n          )}\n          {message.isStreaming && !message.content && (\n            <span className=\"message-bubble__typing\">Thinking...</span>\n          )}\n        </div>\n      ) : (\n        <>\n          <div className=\"message-bubble__content\">\n            {message.content ? ## Report

### What was implemented

Created `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/session.py` with three functions:

1. **`get_session_path(session_id: str) -> Path`** -- Constructs the path to a Claude Agent SDK session JSONL file at `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/<session-id>.jsonl`, where `<encoded-cwd>` replaces `/` with `-` in `os.getcwd()`.

2. **`_is_user_query(entry: dict) -> bool`** -- Distinguishes actual user query entries from tool result entries. Both have `type: "user"`, but user queries have `message.content` as a string while tool results have it as a list.

3. **`truncate_session(session_id: str, user_message_index: int) -> None`** -- Reads the session JSONL file, walks through lines counting user query entries, and truncates the file at the specified user message index. Uses atomic writes via `tempfile.mkstemp()` + `os.replace()` to prevent data corruption. Raises `FileNotFoundError` if the session file doesn't exist and `ValueError` if the user message index is out of range.

### Verification results

- Import check passed: `from app.session import truncate_session, get_session_path` succeeds.
- All functional tests passed:
  - `_is_user_query` correctly identifies user queries vs tool results vs other entry types.
  - `get_session_path` produces the expected path format.
  - `truncate_session` at index 1 correctly keeps lines 0-3 (system + first Q&A + tool result) and removes lines 4-5 (second Q&A).
  - `truncate_session` at index 0 correctly keeps only preceding non-user-query lines.
  - `FileNotFoundError` raised for missing session files.
  - `ValueError` raised for out-of-range indices.

### Files changed

- **Created:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/session.py` (95 lines)

### Self-review findings

- The atomic write pattern with `tempfile.mkstemp` + `os.replace` is robust; the `except BaseException` handler ensures the temp file is cleaned up even on interrupts.
- Empty/malformed JSONL lines are gracefully skipped via `json.JSONDecodeError` handling.
- The function uses `splitlines(keepends=True)` to preserve original line endings when reassembling truncated content.

### Issues or concerns

None. The implementation matches the specification precisely and all edge cases are handled.

## Verification Report

### 1. All 3 functions exist with correct signatures

- `get_session_path(session_id: str) -> Path` -- line 9. **Correct.**
- `_is_user_query(entry: dict) -> bool` -- line 22. **Correct.**
- `truncate_session(session_id: str, user_message_index: int) -> None` -- line 35. **Correct.**

### 2. `_is_user_query` correctly checks type=="user" AND isinstance(content, str)

Lines 28-32: Checks `entry.get("type") != "user"`, then retrieves `message.content`, and returns `isinstance(content, str)`. **Correct.** It also handles missing keys gracefully via `.get()` with defaults.

### 3. `get_session_path` builds the correct path with "/" -> "-" encoding

Lines 17-19: Uses `os.getcwd()`, replaces `"/"` with `"-"`, constructs `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/<session-id>.jsonl`. **Correct.**

### 4. `truncate_session` correctly counts user queries and truncates at the right point

Lines 57-73: Initializes `user_count = 0`, walks through lines, skips empty/malformed lines, calls `_is_user_query()`, and when `user_count == user_message_index`, records the truncation line index and breaks. Otherwise increments `user_count`. Line 80 then takes `lines[:truncation_line]`, which excludes the Nth user query and everything after. **Correct.**

### 5. Atomic write pattern is correct

Lines 83-95: Uses `tempfile.mkstemp()` in the same directory as the target file, writes content via `os.fdopen(fd, "w")`, then calls `os.replace(tmp_path, session_path)`. On failure, cleans up the temp file. **Correct.** The temp file is in the same directory, which is important because `os.replace()` requires source and destination to be on the same filesystem for atomicity.

### 6. Error handling (FileNotFoundError, ValueError) is correct

- Lines 52-53: Raises `FileNotFoundError` if the session file does not exist. **Correct.**
- Lines 75-78: Raises `ValueError` if `truncation_line is None` (i.e., the index was not found). **Correct.**

### 7. No extra/unneeded features were added

The file contains only the three specified functions, necessary imports (`json`, `os`, `tempfile`, `Path`), and a module docstring. The graceful handling of empty/malformed JSONL lines (lines 62-67) is a reasonable defensive measure, not extraneous functionality. The `BaseException` catch with temp file cleanup (lines 89-95) is a standard safety pattern for atomic writes. **No extras found.**

---

## Result: Spec compliant

All three functions exist with the correct signatures. The logic for `_is_user_query`, `get_session_path`, and `truncate_session` all match the specification exactly. Error handling is correct, the atomic write pattern is sound, and no unneeded features were added.

Now I have a thorough understanding of the plan, the design, the implementation, and the surrounding codebase. Here is my review.

---

## Code Review: Session JSONL Truncation Utility

**File:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/session.py`
**Commit:** `7d09567` -- `feat: add session JSONL truncation utility`

---

### What Was Done Well

1. **Plan alignment is strong.** The implementation faithfully delivers all three functions specified in Task 1 of the plan: `truncate_session()`, `_is_user_query()`, and `get_session_path()`. The function signatures, parameters, return types, docstrings, and exception semantics all match the plan and design document.

2. **Atomic write pattern is correct and improved over the plan.** The plan used `tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile` while the implementation uses `tempfile.mkstemp` with an explicit `try/except BaseException` cleanup block. This is a beneficial deviation -- it handles failure cases (disk full, permissions errors) by cleaning up the temp file, preventing orphaned `.jsonl.tmp` files from accumulating. The plan's version had no cleanup on failure.

3. **Defensive JSON parsing.** The implementation adds `try/except json.JSONDecodeError` around `json.loads()` and skips empty lines. The plan's version would crash on any malformed or empty line. This is a worthwhile improvement for robustness against real-world JSONL files that might contain blank lines or partial writes.

4. **Good module-level documentation.** The file opens with a clear module docstring. Each function has well-written docstrings that explain both behavior and the distinction between user queries and tool results.

5. **Clean separation of concerns.** The three functions have clear single responsibilities: path resolution, entry classification, and truncation logic.

---

### Issues

#### Important (should fix)

**1. `get_session_path` uses `os.getcwd()`, which is unreliable in a web server context.**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/session.py`, lines 17-18:

```python
cwd = os.getcwd()
encoded_cwd = cwd.replace("/", "-")
```

The current working directory of a FastAPI process depends on how the server is started and can change at any point. If the server is launched from a different directory, or if something calls `os.chdir()` elsewhere, this path will be wrong. The plan also has this same issue, so this is an inherited flaw.

**Recommendation:** Consider making the working directory configurable via an environment variable or the `config.py` module rather than relying on `os.getcwd()`. For example:

```python
# In config.py
PROJECT_DIR = os.getenv("PROJECT_DIR", os.getcwd())

# In session.py
from app.config import PROJECT_DIR

def get_session_path(session_id: str) -> Path:
    encoded_cwd = PROJECT_DIR.replace("/", "-")
    return Path.home() / ".claude" / "projects" / encoded_cwd / f"{session_id}.jsonl"
```

This makes the behavior deterministic and testable.

**2. No input validation on `session_id`.**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/session.py`, line 50:

```python
session_path = get_session_path(session_id)
```

The `session_id` parameter is used directly in a filesystem path. A malicious or malformed value like `"../../etc/passwd"` or `""` could produce unexpected paths. While the Claude Agent SDK likely produces valid UUIDs, the utility is a public function that will be called from an HTTP endpoint.

**Recommendation:** Add a basic validation check for the `session_id` format. Since Claude session IDs are UUIDs:

```python
import re

_SESSION_ID_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^[a-f0-9\-]{36}$")

def get_session_path(session_id: str) -> Path:
    if not _SESSION_ID_PATTERN.match(session_id):
        raise ValueError(f"Invalid session_id format: {session_id}")
    ...
```

**3. No unit tests.**

The plan only specifies a basic import check (`python -c "from backend.app.session import ..."`), but this module contains non-trivial logic -- counting user messages, handling edge cases in JSON parsing, and atomic file operations. Without tests, regressions in the truncation logic or the `_is_user_query` classifier could go undetected.

**Recommendation:** Create `backend/tests/test_session.py` with tests covering:
- Truncating at various user message indices (first, middle, last)
- Correct distinction between user queries and tool results in `_is_user_query`
- `FileNotFoundError` when session file is missing
- `ValueError` when user message index is out of range
- Empty lines and malformed JSON lines are handled gracefully
- Atomic write behavior (file is not corrupted on success)

These can use `tmp_path` fixtures with fabricated JSONL content -- no real Claude session needed.

#### Suggestions (nice to have)

**4. The `truncation_line` variable type annotation uses Python 3.10+ union syntax.**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/session.py`, line 57:

```python
truncation_line: int | None = None
```

This is consistent with the existing codebase (e.g., `session_id: str | None = None` in `chat.py`), so it is not a compatibility problem in this project. Just noting that if the project ever needs to support Python 3.9, this would need `Optional[int]` from `typing`.

**5. The error message in `FileNotFoundError` exposes the full filesystem path.**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/session.py`, line 53:

```python
raise FileNotFoundError(f"Session file not found: {session_path}")
```

The plan's version used `f"Session file not found: {session_id}"` (just the ID). The current version leaks the home directory and project path structure in the error message, which could be exposed through the HTTP 404 response in the future endpoint. The plan's approach of only including `session_id` is slightly better from a security standpoint.

**6. Consider adding a `user_message_index` bounds check for negative values.**

A caller could pass `user_message_index=-1`, which would never match the counting logic and would raise the generic `ValueError`. Adding an explicit early check would provide a clearer error:

```python
if user_message_index < 0:
    raise ValueError(f"user_message_index must be non-negative, got {user_message_index}")
```

**7. The function name `get_session_path` was renamed from the plan's `_get_session_path`.**

The plan used a private underscore-prefixed name `_get_session_path`, while the design document's code snippet also used the private convention. The implementation makes it public as `get_session_path`. This is an intentional deviation that makes sense -- the function is imported and used by the route handler in later tasks. The plan's Task 1 code block also shows it as public `get_session_path`, so this is actually consistent with the plan (the design doc had the underscore prefix, but the plan corrected it).

---

### Plan Deviation Summary

| Deviation | Type | Assessment |
|-----------|------|------------|
| `mkstemp` + cleanup block instead of `NamedTemporaryFile` | Improvement | Beneficial -- prevents orphaned temp files on failure |
| Empty line skipping and `JSONDecodeError` handling | Improvement | Beneficial -- more robust against real-world data |
| `read_text().splitlines(keepends=True)` instead of `f.readlines()` | Equivalent | Functionally identical, stylistically fine |
| Error message includes full path instead of just session ID | Minor regression | Exposes filesystem structure, should use session ID only |
| No tests beyond import check | Gap | Matches the plan, but the plan was minimal on testing |

---

### Summary

The implementation is solid and closely aligned with the plan. The code is clean, well-documented, and includes meaningful improvements over the plan (defensive JSON parsing, better temp file cleanup). ### Changes Made

**File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/config.py`**

Added `PROJECT_DIR` configuration at line 14:
```python
PROJECT_DIR = os.getenv("PROJECT_DIR", os.getcwd())
```
This allows the working directory used for session path construction to be overridden via the `PROJECT_DIR` environment variable, instead of always relying on `os.getcwd()` at call time.

**File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/session.py`**

Three changes were made:

1. **Configurable CWD (Fix 1)** -- Added `from app.config import PROJECT_DIR` and replaced `os.getcwd()` with `PROJECT_DIR` in `get_session_path`. The directory is now determined once at import time from config rather than calling `os.getcwd()` on every invocation.

2. **Session ID validation (Fix 2)** -- Added `import re` and a module-level compiled pattern `_SESSION_ID_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^[a-f0-9\-]{36}$")`. At the top of `get_session_path`, the session ID is validated against this pattern. If it does not match (e.g., contains `../` or other path traversal characters), a `ValueError` is raised immediately.

3. **Safer error message (Fix 3)** -- Changed the `FileNotFoundError` in `truncate_session` from exposing the full filesystem path (`{session_path}`) to only including the session ID (`{session_id}`). This avoids leaking internal path structure in error responses.

### Verification

The import check (`from app.session import truncate_session, get_session_path`) printed `ok`, confirming no import errors.

- **`POST /api/chat/delete`** -- Accepts a `ChatDeleteRequest` (with `session_id` and `user_message_index`). It truncates the session JSONL file at the specified index and returns `{"ok": True}`.

Here is the summary of what was implemented:

---

## What Was Implemented

All 7 changes from the task description were applied to `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/src/AgentContext.tsx`:

1. **Updated import** (Change 1): Added `runAgentEditLoop` and `deleteAgentMessage` imports from `./agent/agentService`.

2. **Updated `AgentContextValue` interface** (Change 2): Added `editMessage` and `deleteMessage` method signatures.

3. **Updated default context** (Change 3): Added `editMessage: () => {}` and `deleteMessage: () => {}` default values.

4. **Added `getUserMessageIndex` helper** (Change 4): A `useCallback` that counts user messages up to a given index, converting from the flat message array index to the user-turn index needed by the backend API.

5. **Added `editMessage` callback** (Change 5): A full streaming callback that mirrors `sendMessage` but calls `runAgentEditLoop` instead. It truncates messages at `messageIndex` before appending the new user message and assistant placeholder, then streams the response with the same `onTextChunk`/`onThinkingDone`/`onToolCall`/`onToolResult`/`onDone`/`onError` handlers.

6. **Added `deleteMessage` callback** (Change 6): Calls `deleteAgentMessage` on the backend to truncate the session JSONL file, then slices the local messages array. Resets the session ID if all messages are deleted.

7. **Updated provider value** (Change 7): Added `editMessage` and `deleteMessage` to the context provider's `value` prop.

## TypeScript Compilation Results

`npx tsc --noEmit` passed with **zero errors**.

## Files Changed

- `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/src/AgentContext.tsx` -- Primary file with all 7 changes (grew from 243 lines to ~429 lines)
- `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/src/useAgent.ts` -- New file created by linter auto-fix, extracting the `useAgent` hook
- `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/src/components/AgentPanel.tsx` -- Import path updated by linter (from `../AgentContext` to `../useAgent`)
- `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/src/components/ChatInput.tsx` -- Import path updated by linter (same)

## Self-Review Findings

- The linter automatically refactored the `useAgent` hook out of `AgentContext.tsx` into its own `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/src/useAgent.ts` file and updated the import paths in `AgentPanel.tsx` and `ChatInput.tsx`. It also changed `const AgentContext` to `export const AgentContext`. These are correct and necessary changes -- the `useAgent` hook is consumed via the new file, and `AgentContext` is now exported so `useAgent.ts` can import it.
- The `editMessage` callback correctly mirrors `sendMessage`'s streaming pattern but uses `runAgentEditLoop` and truncates messages via `prev.slice(0, messageIndex)`.
- The `deleteMessage` callback properly calls the backend API before updating local state, and handles the edge case of deleting all messages by resetting `sessionIdRef`.

## Issues or Concerns

None. All changes compile cleanly and align with the task specification. The linter's automatic refactoring of `useAgent` into its own file is a clean separation that doesn't affect functionality.

Here is the summary of what was done:

---

## What was implemented

Added edit/delete hover actions and inline editing UI to the `MessageBubble` component. When a user hovers over their own message (and the agent is not streaming), pencil (edit) and trash (delete) icon buttons appear in the message header. Clicking edit opens an inline textarea pre-filled with the message content, with "Save & Resend" and "Cancel" buttons. Clicking delete shows a confirmation banner ("Delete this and all following messages?") with Delete/Cancel options. Keyboard shortcuts are supported: Enter to save, Escape to cancel.

## TypeScript compilation results

`npx tsc --noEmit` passed with zero errors.

## Files changed

1. **`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/src/components/AgentPanel.tsx`** -- Updated `messages.map` to pass `messageIndex` as a prop to each `MessageBubble`.

2. **`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/src/components/MessageBubble.tsx`** -- Added imports for `useState`, `useRef`, `useEffect`, and `useAgent`. Replaced the `MessageBubble` component to accept `messageIndex`, manage editing/delete-confirmation state, and render hover action buttons, inline edit textarea, and delete confirmation UI for user messages. The `ThinkingBlock` helper and its associated function remain unchanged.

3. **`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/frontend/src/components/MessageBubble.css`** -- Added `display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 4px;` to the existing `.message-bubble__header` rule. Appended new CSS rules for hover actions (`.message-bubble__actions`, `.message-bubble__action-btn`), edit mode (`.message-bubble__edit-mode`, `.message-bubble__edit-textarea`, `.message-bubble__edit-btn`), and delete confirmation (`.message-bubble__confirm-delete`, `.message-bubble__confirm-btn`).

## Self-review findings

- The `useAgent` import correctly points to `'../useAgent'` (not `'../AgentContext'`), matching the project's refactored structure.
- The existing `.message-bubble__header` CSS rule was updated in-place rather than duplicated, avoiding style conflicts.
- The `editMessage` and `deleteMessage` functions are properly destructured from `useAgent()`, and `isStreaming` is used to conditionally hide action buttons during streaming -- preventing user interaction while the agent is responding.
- No issues or concerns identified.

Perfect! Now let me provide you with the comprehensive findings:

## Summary: "Fatal error in message reader" Debug Analysis

I've thoroughly analyzed the Claude Agent SDK code to understand what happens when you resume a truncated session. Here's the complete picture:

### **1. WHERE THE ERROR COMES FROM**

**File:** `claude_agent_sdk/_internal/query.py`, Line 221

```python
except Exception as e:
    logger.error(f"Fatal error in message reader: {e}")
```

This is a generic exception handler in the `Query._read_messages()` method that runs as a background async task. It catches **any unhandled exception** from the message reading loop.

### **2. ROOT CAUSE: THE ERROR CHAIN**

When you resume a truncated session, here's what happens:

```
Your app calls: client.query(message, session_id=<id>)
    ↓
SDK creates subprocess command: ["claude", ..., "--resume", "<session_id>", ...]
    ↓
Claude CLI starts and tries to load: ~/.claude/projects/.../< session_id>.jsonl
    ↓
IF JSONL IS TRUNCATED/MALFORMED:
    ├─ CLI fails to parse the incomplete JSON
    ├─ CLI exits with returncode=1 (non-zero)
    ├─ stderr contains error details (but not captured)
    └─ stdout is empty/closed
    ↓
SDK's _read_messages_impl() detects exit code != 0 (subprocess_cli.py:579)
    ↓
Raises: ProcessError("Command failed with exit code 1", ...)  [Line 585]
    ↓
Caught by Query._read_messages() [query.py:220]
    ↓
Logged as: "Fatal error in message reader: <ProcessError>" [query.py:221]
```

### **3. HOW THE RESUME FLAG IS HANDLED**

In `subprocess_cli.py`, lines 227-228:
```python
if self._options.resume:
    cmd.extend(["--resume", self._options.resume])
```

The session ID is passed directly. Claude CLI then looks for the session file at:
```
~/.claude/projects/-Users-cyyeh-Desktop-duckdb-data-agent/<session_id>.jsonl
```

### **4. SESSION DIRECTORY STRUCTURE**

Your session directory contains:
- **JSONL files:** `<session-id>.jsonl` - The main session state (JSONL format, one JSON object per line)
- **Session directories:** `<session-id>/` with subdirectories:
  - `subagents/` - Sub-agent sessions
  - `tool-results/` - Tool execution results
- **Memory directory:** Empty in your case

The session file MUST have valid JSON on every line. If the last line is incomplete, the CLI parser fails.

### **5. WHY STDERR IS NOT CAPTURED (THE BIG PROBLEM)**

In `subprocess_cli.py`, lines 360-367:
```python
should_pipe_stderr = (
    self._options.stderr is not None  # ← NO CALLBACK PROVIDED
    or "debug-to-stderr" in self._options.extra_args
)
stderr_dest = PIPE if should_pipe_stderr else None  # ← NONE!
```

In your `app/agent.py`, lines 68-77:
```python
options = ClaudeAgentOptions(
    model=ANTHROPIC_MODEL,
    system_prompt=build_system_prompt(),
    mcp_servers={"duckdb": duckdb_server},
    allowed_tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
    permission_mode="bypassPermissions",
    max_turns=20,
    include_partial_messages=True,
    # ← NO stderr= parameter
    **({"resume": session_id} if session_id else {}),
)
```

**Result:** When the CLI subprocess writes error messages to stderr, they're completely discarded. You only get the generic "Command failed with exit code 1" message.

### **6. THE ACTUAL ERROR MESSAGE PROBLEM**

At `subprocess_cli.py`, line 580:
```python
ProcessError(
    f"Command failed with exit code {returncode}",
    exit_code=returncode,
    stderr="Check stderr output for details",  # ← HARDCODED!
)
```

The stderr parameter is a **hardcoded string** that doesn't contain the actual subprocess stderr. This is why the error message is so unhelpful.

### **7. SESSION FILE STATE**

I verified your most recent session file:
- **File:** `f507e2f1-60b6-4deb-930d-1207c5ccdfab.jsonl`
- **Size:** 3.4MB
- **Lines:** 1,166 (all valid JSON)
- **Message types:** user, assistant, progress, system, file-history-snapshot, queue-operation, stream_event

This particular file is well-formed and not truncated. The issue would occur if the last JSON line was incomplete (missing closing brace).

### **8. HOW TO DEBUG & FIX**

**Immediate fix - Capture stderr to see the real error:**

Add a stderr callback to `agent.py`:
```python
stderr_output = []

options = ClaudeAgentOptions(
    model=ANTHROPIC_MODEL,
    system_prompt=build_system_prompt(),
    mcp_servers={"duckdb": duckdb_server},
    allowed_tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
    permission_mode="bypassPermissions",
    max_turns=20,
    include_partial_messages=True,
    stderr=lambda line: stderr_output.append(line),  # ← ADD THIS
    **({"resume": session_id} if session_id else {}),
)

# Then in your exception handler:
except Exception as e:
    if stderr_output:
        error_detail = " | ".join(stderr_output)
        yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': f'{str(e)} - CLI stderr: {error_detail}'})}\n\n"
```

**Prevention - Validate session before resuming:**

```python
import json
from pathlib import Path

def validate_session_jsonl(session_id: str) -> bool:
    """Validate that session JSONL file is not truncated."""
    path = Path.home() / ".claude" / "projects" / "-Users-cyyeh-Desktop-duckdb-data-agent" / f"{session_id}.jsonl"
    try:
        with open(path) as f:
            for line in f:
                json.loads(line.strip())
        return True
    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
        logger.error(f"Session file {session_id} has invalid JSON: {e}")
        return False
    except FileNotFoundError:
        logger.error(f"Session file {session_id} not found")
        return False

# In stream_chat:
if session_id and not validate_session_jsonl(session_id):
    raise ValueError(f"Cannot resume: session file {session_id} is corrupted or truncated")
```

### **Key Files Referenced**

- `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/claude_agent_sdk/_internal/transport/subprocess_cli.py`
  - Lines 227-228: Resume flag handling
  - Lines 519-585: Message reading and exit code detection
  - Lines 360-388: stderr piping setup

- `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/claude_agent_sdk/_internal/query.py`
  - Lines 172-231: Message reading loop with exception handler
  - Line 221: The error log statement

- `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/agent.py`
  - Lines 68-77: ClaudeAgentOptions configuration (needs stderr callback added)

This analysis explains exactly why the error occurs and how to capture the real error details from the Claude CLI subprocess.

# Run both backend and frontend concurrently
dev:

	cd backend && poetry run uvicorn app.main:app --reload --port 8000 & \
	cd frontend && npm run dev & \
	wait

backend:

frontend:

# Install all dependencies
install: install-backend install-frontend

install-backend:

> AGENT

**Root cause confirmed.** Every web app session starts with `queue-operation, queue-operation, user, assistant...`. When editing the first message (index 0), our truncation removes everything from line 2 onward, leaving only the two `queue-operation` entries. The Claude CLI can't resume a session with no actual user/assistant messages — it crashes with exit code 1.

**Phase 2: Pattern Analysis**

The JSONL truncation approach is fundamentally fragile — the session format is an internal implementation detail of the CLI, and queue-operation entries, parentUuid chains, etc. make direct manipulation unreliable.

**Phase 3: Hypothesis**

The reliable fix is the "fresh session with context" approach we discussed earlier as the alternative:
1. Don't truncate the JSONL at all
2. Start a **fresh** session (no `--resume`)
3. Include the conversation history before the edit point in the **system prompt**
4. The new session becomes the active session going forward

For **delete**: no backend call needed — just truncate frontend messages and clear `sessionIdRef`. The next message starts a fresh session.

  "new_string": "def build_system_prompt(conversation_history: list[dict] | None = None) -> str:\n    tables = db.list_tables()\n    prompt = \"\"\"You are a helpful data analyst assistant working with a DuckDB database.\nYou can execute SQL queries using the execute_sql tool to answer questions about the user's data.\n\nGuidelines:\n- Write clear, efficient DuckDB SQL queries\n- When exploring data, start with small queries (use LIMIT)\n- Explain your findings in plain language after getting results\n- If a query fails, try to fix it and retry\n- Use double quotes for table and column names that might conflict with reserved words\n\"\"\"\n    if not tables:\n        prompt += \"\\nNo tables are currently loaded. Ask the user to upload a CSV file first.\"\n    else:\n        prompt += \"\\nCurrently loaded tables:\\n\"\n        for table in tables:\n            prompt += f'\\nTable: \"{table[\"name\"]}\" ({table[\"rowCount\"]} rows)\\nColumns:\\n'\n            for col in table[\"columns\"]:\n                prompt += f'  - \"{col[\"name\"]}\" ({col[\"type\"]})\\n'\n\n    if conversation_history:\n        prompt += \"\\n\\nPrevious conversation (for context, the user is now editing a message):\\n\"\n        for entry in conversation_history:\n            role = entry.get(\"role\", \"user\").capitalize()\n            content = entry.get(\"content\", \"\")\n            prompt += f\"\\n{role}: {content}\\n\"\n\n    return prompt"

{
  "file_path": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend/app/routes/chat.py",
  "content": "from fastapi import APIRouter\nfrom fastapi.responses import StreamingResponse\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel\n\nfrom app.agent import stream_chat\n\nrouter = APIRouter(prefix=\"/api\", tags=[\"chat\"])\n\n\nclass ChatRequest(BaseModel):\n    message: str\n    session_id: str | None = None\n\n\nclass ChatEditRequest(BaseModel):\n    new_message: str\n    conversation_history: list[dict] = []\n\n\n@router.post(\"/chat\")\nasync def chat(request: ChatRequest):\n    return StreamingResponse(\n        stream_chat(request.message, request.session_id),\n        media_type=\"text/event-stream\",\n        headers={\n            \"Cache-Control\": \"no-cache\",\n            \"Connection\": \"keep-alive\",\n            \"X-Accel-Buffering\": \"no\",\n        },\n    )\n\n\n@router.post(\"/chat/edit\")\nasync def chat_edit(request: ChatEditRequest):\n    \"\"\"Edit a message: start a fresh session with conversation history as context.\"\"\"\n    return StreamingResponse(\n        stream_chat(\n            request.new_message,\n            session_id=None,\n            conversation_history=request.conversation_history,\n        ),\n        media_type=\"text/event-stream\",\n        headers={\n            \"Cache-Control\": \"no-cache\",\n            \"Connection\": \"keep-alive\",\n            \"X-Accel-Buffering\": \"no\",\n        },\n    )\n"
}

  "old_string": "  const editMessage = useCallback(\n    async (messageIndex: number, newContent: string) => {\n      if (isStreaming) return;\n      if (!sessionIdRef.current) return;\n\n      const userMsgIndex = getUserMessageIndex(messageIndex);\n\n      const assistantId = generateId();\n      assistantIdRef.current = assistantId;",
  "new_string": "  const editMessage = useCallback(\n    async (messageIndex: number, newContent: string) => {\n      if (isStreaming) return;\n\n      // Build conversation history from messages before the edit point\n      const conversationHistory: { role: string; content: string }[] = [];\n      for (let i = 0; i < messageIndex; i++) {\n        const msg = messages[i];\n        if (msg.role === 'user' || msg.role === 'assistant') {\n          conversationHistory.push({ role: msg.role, content: msg.content });\n        }\n      }\n\n      const assistantId = generateId();\n      assistantIdRef.current = assistantId;"
}

```
     1→# DuckDB Data Agent
     2→
     3→> **Note:** This project is built with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/product/claude-code)(using Opus 4.6 and [superpowers](https://github.com/obra/superpowers)) by more than 95% and human-reviewed by author.
     4→
     5→[![Deploy to Render](https://render.com/images/deploy-to-render-button.svg)](https://render.com/deploy)
     6→
     7→A SQL playground with an AI-powered data analysis agent. Upload CSV files, write SQL queries, or ask questions in plain English — powered by [DuckDB](https://duckdb.org/) on a lightweight [FastAPI](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/) backend with a React frontend.
     8→
     9→## Features
    10→
    11→### SQL Playground
    12→
    13→- **DuckDB SQL engine** — Fast, in-process analytical database on the backend
    14→- **CSV file upload** — Drag-and-drop or click to import CSV files (up to 500 MB) with automatic schema detection
    15→- **Sample dataset** — One-click load of the Titanic dataset to get started quickly
    16→- **SQL query editor** — Write and execute queries with Ctrl/Cmd+Enter
    17→- **Interactive results** — Sortable columns, per-column filters, and global search across results
    18→- **EXPLAIN support** — Markdown-rendered output for `EXPLAIN` and `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` queries
    19→- **Table sidebar** — Collapsible panel to browse tables, inspect columns, and view types
    20→
    21→### AI Agent
    22→
    23→- **Natural language queries** — Ask questions about your data in plain English; the agent writes and executes SQL for you
    24→- **Streaming responses** — Real-time token streaming powered by Claude via the [Anthropic Agent SDK](https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python)
    25→- **Visible reasoning** — Collapsible thinking block shows the agent's intermediate steps and SQL queries
    26→- **Inline results** — Query results rendered inline within the conversation
    27→- **Privacy-conscious** — Requires an Anthropic API key stored in a server-side `.env` file; your data and key are never sent anywhere besides the Anthropic API
    28→- **Langfuse observability** (optional) — Built-in [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/) tracing for monitoring agent interactions, with a one-click dashboard link in the UI
    29→
    30→## Getting Started
    31→
    32→### Prerequisites
    33→
    34→- [Node.js](https://nodejs.org/) 20+
    35→- [Python](https://www.python.org/) 3.12+
    36→- [Poetry](https://python-poetry.org/)
    37→
    38→### Installation
    39→
    40→```bash
    41→make install
    42→```
    43→
    44→Or install frontend and backend separately:
    45→
    46→```bash
    47→cd frontend && npm install
    48→cd backend && poetry install
    49→```
    50→
    51→### Configuration
    52→
    53→Copy the example environment file and add your Anthropic API key:
    54→
    55→```bash
    56→cp backend/.env.example backend/.env
    57→```
    58→
    59→Edit `backend/.env` and set your key:
    60→
    61→```
    62→ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-...
    63→ANTHROPIC_MODEL=sonnet    # optional, defaults to sonnet
    64→```
    65→
    66→> Both variables are only needed for the AI agent. The SQL playground works without them, but both require the backend running.
    67→
    68→#### Langfuse (optional)
    69→
    70→To enable agent tracing with [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/), add these to `backend/.env`:
    71→
    72→```
    73→LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY=pk-lf-...
    74→LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY=sk-lf-...
    75→LANGFUSE_BASE_URL=https://cloud.langfuse.com   # optional, defaults to cloud
    76→```
    77→
    78→When configured, every agent conversation is traced (LLM turns, tool calls, SQL execution) and a **Langfuse Traces** button appears in the agent panel header linking to your dashboard. When not configured, tracing is disabled with zero overhead.
    79→
    80→### Development
    81→
    82→Start both the frontend and backend:
    83→
    84→```bash
    85→make dev
    86→```
    87→
    88→Or run them separately:
    89→
    90→```bash
    91→make frontend   # http://localhost:5173
    92→make backend    # http://localhost:8000
    93→```
    94→
    95→Open http://localhost:5173 to use the app. The Vite dev server proxies `/api` requests to the backend automatically.
    96→
    97→## Production Build and Deployment
    98→
    99→The project ships as a single Docker image that bundles the React frontend and FastAPI backend. A multi-stage `Dockerfile` builds the frontend, then copies the output into the backend's static directory.
   100→
   101→### Build and run locally
   102→
   103→```bash
   104→docker build -t duckdb-data-agent .
   105→docker run -p 10000:10000 \
   106→  -e ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-... \
   107→  -e LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY=pk-lf-... \
   108→  -e LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY=sk-lf-... \
   109→  duckdb-data-agent
   110→```
   111→
   112→Open http://localhost:10000 to use the app.
   113→
   114→### Deploy to Render
   115→
   116→A `render.yaml` is included for one-click deployment on [Render](https://render.com/):
   117→
   118→1. Push this repo to GitHub.
   119→2. In Render, create a new **Blueprint** and connect the repo.
   120→3. Set the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` environment variable in the Render dashboard. Optionally set `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` to override the default model (`sonnet`). To enable Langfuse tracing, also set `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY` and `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY`.
   121→
   122→Render will build the Docker image and deploy it automatically on every push to `main`.
   123→
   124→## Project Structure
   125→
   126→```
   127→├── frontend/               # React frontend
   128→│   ├── public/             #   Static assets (Langfuse icon)
   129→│   ├── src/
   130→│   │   ├── components/     #   UI components (editor, results, sidebar, chat)
   131→│   │   ├── agent/          #   Agent service (SSE event handling)
   132→│   │   ├── AgentContext.tsx #   Agent state management
   133→│   │   └── types.ts        #   Shared TypeScript interfaces
   134→│   ├── index.html          #   HTML entry point
   135→│   ├── package.json        #   npm config
   136→│   └── vite.config.ts      #   Vite bundler config
   137→├── backend/                # FastAPI backend
   138→│   └── app/
   139→│       ├── main.py         #   App setup & CORS
   140→│       ├── config.py       #   Environment variables (API key, model)
   141→│       ├── database.py     #   DuckDB connection & query execution
   142→│       ├── agent.py        #   Agent loop & SSE streaming
   143→│       ├── tracing.py      #   Langfuse client wrapper & initialization
   144→│       ├── tools.py        #   Agent SDK tool definitions (execute_sql)
   145→│       ├── data/           #   Sample datasets (titanic.csv)
   146→│       └── routes/         #   API endpoints (tables, query, chat, langfuse status)
   147→├── Dockerfile              # Multi-stage production build
   148→├── render.yaml             # Render deployment config
   149→└── Makefile                # Dev commands (install, dev, clean)
   150→```
   151→
   152→## Tech Stack
   153→
   154→**Frontend**
   155→- [React](https://react.dev/) 18 + [TypeScript](https://www.typescriptlang.org/)
   156→- [Vite](https://vite.dev/)
   157→
   158→**Backend**
   159→- [FastAPI](https://fastapi.tiangolo.com/) + [Uvicorn](https://www.uvicorn.org/)
   160→- [DuckDB](https://duckdb.org/) (Python)
   161→- [Anthropic Agent SDK](https://github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-python)
   162→- [Langfuse](https://langfuse.com/) (optional, for observability)
   163→
   164→## License
   165→
   166→[MIT](LICENSE.txt)
   167→
```
